# Gaza genocide

This article is about the ongoing genocide by Israel in Gaza since 2023. For broader allegations of genocide against Palestinians by Israel, see [Palestinian genocide allegations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_genocide_allegations).

The **Gaza genocide** is the ongoing,[^cite_note-20] [intentional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intent_and_incitement_in_the_Gaza_genocide), and systematic destruction of the [Palestinian people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_people) in the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) carried out by [Israel](9282173.md) during the [Gaza war](74998519.md). It encompasses [mass killings](135327.md), the targeted killing of children (who account for 30% of those killed),[^cite_note-Holmes-21][^cite_note-22][^cite_note-ohchrjune26-23] deliberate [starvation](75828824.md), infliction of serious bodily and mental harm, and [prevention of births](75314000.md#Attacks_in_the_Gaza_Strip). Other acts include [blockading](19224310.md), [destroying civilian infrastructure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attacks_on_protected_zones_and_civilians_in_Gaza), destroying [healthcare facilities](75314000.md), killing [healthcare workers](75657910.md) and [aid-seekers](80075155.md), causing mass [forced displacement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip_evacuations), committing [sexual violence](76149089.md), and destroying [educational](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attacks_on_schools_during_the_Gaza_war), [religious](77727480.md), and [cultural sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destruction_of_cultural_heritage_during_the_Gaza_war).[^cite_note-UNCommissionGenocide-24] The genocide has been recognised by [a United Nations special committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Committee_to_Investigate_Israeli_Practices_Affecting_the_Human_Rights_of_the_Palestinian_People)[^cite_note-UNCommitteeHeadGenocide-25] and [commission of inquiry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_UNHRC_Commission_of_Inquiry_report_on_Gaza_genocide),[^cite_note-UNCommissionGenocide-24] the [International Association of Genocide Scholars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Association_of_Genocide_Scholars),[^cite_note-IAGSReuters-26][^cite_note-IAGSResolution-27] [multiple human rights groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_humanitarian_and_human_rights_groups_accusing_Israel_of_genocide_in_Gaza),[^cite_note-33] [state governments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_genocide_recognition), numerous [genocide studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_studies) and [international law](8195726.md) scholars,[^cite_note-growing_agreement-34][^cite_note-genocide_int_law_scholars-35] and other experts.[^cite_note-36]

As of September 2026, at least 73,400 people in Gaza [had been killed](75130785.md).[^cite_note-snapshot-6][^cite_note-theguardian-70000-18] The vast majority of the victims were civilians,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESchwarz2024-37][^cite_note-FOOTNOTETanteshGraham-Harrison2024-38] and around 50% were [women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_the_Gaza_war) and [children](75987631.md).[^cite_note-Spagat_2024a-39][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraham-Harrison2024a-40] Compared to other recent global conflicts, the numbers of known [deaths of journalists](75146557.md), humanitarian and [health workers](75657910.md), and children are among the highest.[^cite_note-41] Thousands more uncounted bodies are thought to be under the rubble of destroyed buildings.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETanteshGraham-Harrison2024-38][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMassoudFick2023-42] A study in the medical journal *[The Lancet](463899.md)* estimated that traumatic injury deaths were undercounted by June 2024, while noting an even larger potential death toll with indirect deaths included.[^cite_note-43] The number of injured is greater than 174,000.[^cite_note-snapshot-6][^cite_note-Burke_2025-19][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGómez-UgarteChenAcostaBasellini202513-44] Gaza has the most child [amputees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amputees) per capita in the world;[^cite_note-Amputee_stats-45] the Gaza war caused more than 21,000 children to be [disabled](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physical_disability).[^cite_note-Guardian_3_September_2025-46] In July 2025, 18,457 children were listed among the war dead by health authorities in Gaza, excluding indirect deaths and those buried under rubble.[^cite_note-47]

[An Israeli blockade](75013719.md) heavily contributed to [starvation and confirmed famine](75828824.md). In August 2025, about 641,000 people experienced [catastrophic levels of food insecurity](16431152.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIPC_Report_August20251-48][^cite_note-49] Early in the conflict, Israel cut off Gaza's water and electricity. It later partially restored the water.[^cite_note-50] As of May 2024, 84% of Gaza's health centres have been [destroyed or damaged](75314000.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENeumanBabaWood2024-51] Israel also [destroyed numerous cultural heritage sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destruction_of_cultural_heritage_during_the_Gaza_war), including all 12 of Gaza's universities, and 80% of its schools.[^cite_note-scholasticide-52][^cite_note-53] Over 1.9 million Palestinians—85% of Gaza's population—were forcibly displaced.[^cite_note-54] Israel's bombing also caused [severe environmental devastation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_impact_of_the_Gaza_war) across the territory.[^cite_note-Ahmed_et_al.-5]

In December 2023, the government of [South Africa](17416221.md) instituted proceedings, *[South Africa v. Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_v._Israel)*, against Israel at the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) (ICJ), alleging a violation of the [Genocide Convention](143988.md).[^cite_note-ICJ-2023-12-29-55] In January 2024, the court ordered Israel to take all measures within its power to prevent the commission of acts of genocide, to prevent and punish [incitement to genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incitement_to_genocide), and to allow basic [humanitarian service, aid and supplies](434679.md) into Gaza.[^cite_note-ICJruling-56][^cite_note-57][^cite_note-Casciani_2024a-58] The court later ordered Israel to increase humanitarian aid into Gaza and to halt its [Rafah offensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive).[^cite_note-Casciani_2024b-59][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarsiSiddiquiMotamedi2024-60] Israel did not fully comply with the court's orders.[^cite_note-Amnesty_International_Blockade-61][^cite_note-Human_Rights_Watch_Blockade-62][^cite_note-63]

Palestinians have emphasized the relationship between the Gaza genocide and [Zionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionism) [political violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionist_political_violence), describing it as a continuation of the [Nakba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakba) and its [ongoing effects](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ongoing_Nakba). Israel and its supporters maintain that its actions [do not constitute genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_genocide_denial),[^cite_note-Casciani_2024a-58][^cite_note-64] that they are a response to the [October 7 attacks](74998317.md), and that Israel's aim has been to destroy [Hamas](13913.md) and free [Israeli hostages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_hostages).[^cite_note-UN_Commission_of_Inquiry_report_2025_64-65][^cite_note-Casciani_2024a-58][^cite_note-TOI3-11-23-66] There is increasing consensus among genocide and [international legal scholars on the genocide assessment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academic_and_legal_responses_to_the_Gaza_genocide),[^cite_note-growing_agreement-34] although some academics [and countries](75736183.md#In_opposition) challenge it.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMoses20251,_10–13-67][^cite_note-Ayad_2025-68][^cite_note-ICJ_2026_6-69]

## Background

Further information: [Background of the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Background_of_the_Gaza_war) and [Israeli–Palestinian conflict](46216.md)



A September 2023 [United Nations](31769.md) map of Gaza, showing its [border barrier](478337.md) and checkpoints into Israel and Egypt and Israel's [Maritime Exclusion Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maritime_Exclusion_Zone), illustrating the geographic scope of the preexisting blockade and the physical constraints on movement and access.

The [Israeli occupation of the Gaza Strip](76465951.md) began in the [1967 Arab–Israeli War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1967_Arab%E2%80%93Israeli_War). In 2005, Israel [withdrew its ground forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_disengagement_from_the_Gaza_Strip) in the context of the [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords) and the [Second Intifada](71717.md).[^cite_note-70] The [International Court of Justice](14918.md) subsequently issued an [advisory opinion](72240449.md) that despite the withdrawal, Israel was still illegally [occupying the Gaza Strip](76465951.md) due to [its blockade of the territory](19224310.md).[^cite_note-71]

Since 2007, the [Gaza Strip](12047.md) has been governed by [Hamas](13913.md), an [Islamist](15012.md) militant group, while the [West Bank](33209.md) remained under the control of the [Palestinian Authority](24093.md). After Hamas took over, Israel intensified its [blockade of the Gaza Strip](19224310.md), citing security concerns;[^cite_note-72][^cite_note-Meakem-73] international rights groups have called the blockade a form of [collective punishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_punishment).[^cite_note-74][^cite_note-75][^cite_note-76] [UNRWA](32270.md) reported that, due to the blockade, 81% of Gazans were living below the poverty level in 2023, with 63% food insecure and dependent on international assistance.[^cite_note-77][^cite_note-78] Since 2007, Israel and Hamas (and other Palestinian militias in Gaza) have engaged in conflict,[^cite_note-Meakem-73][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKaneCohenShamirScher2023-79][^cite_note-80] including four wars in [2008–2009](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_War_\(2008%E2%80%932009\)), [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Gaza_War), [2014](43243199.md), and [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Israel%E2%80%93Palestine_crisis).[^cite_note-81][^cite_note-82]

On 7 October 2023 Hamas led [an attack into Israel](74998317.md) from Gaza,[^cite_note-83][^cite_note-84][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw20241-85] killing at least 1,139[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESemerdjian2024a1-86][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMarsiSiddiqui2024-87][^cite_note-94] people, most of them [civilians](75007485.md).[^cite_note-F24231215-95] The attack included grave acts of violence, including [sexual violence](75436869.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem20256-96] During the attack, Palestinian militant groups [abducted 251 people from Israel](75025451.md) to the [Gaza Strip](12047.md).[^cite_note-97] [Israel responded](75523996.md) with a highly destructive[^cite_note-98] [bombing campaign](76139149.md) followed by an [invasion of the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Invasion_of_the_Gaza_Strip) on 27 October.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESegalDaniele20246-99]

Hamas officials said the attack was a response to [Israeli occupation of the West Bank](2781576.md), desecration of the [Al-Aqsa Mosque](65759.md), the [blockade of Gaza](19224310.md), [Israeli settler violence](47246246.md) against Palestinians, and the [detention of thousands of Palestinians](18158609.md), whom Hamas sought to release by taking Israeli hostages.[^cite_note-100][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcKernanMichaelsonGraham-HarrisonKierszenbaum2023-101][^cite_note-102] Numerous commentators have identified Israeli occupation as a cause of the war.[^cite_note-103] Several human rights organisations, including [Amnesty International](18947898.md),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAmnesty_International2022266–267-104] [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem),[^cite_note-105] and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md),[^cite_note-106] have [likened the Israeli occupation to apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_apartheid); Israel's supporters dispute this characterisation.[^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108] A July 2024 [advisory opinion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Advisory_opinion) by the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) [affirmed the occupation as illegal](72240449.md) and said it violated the [International Convention on the Elimination of All Forms of Racial Discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Convention_on_the_Elimination_of_All_Forms_of_Racial_Discrimination).[^cite_note-109]

The Israeli government has said that the military actions it has undertaken are in response to the [October 7 attacks](74998317.md) and sought to destroy Hamas, overthrow its [governance of the Gaza Strip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governance_of_the_Gaza_Strip), and free Israeli hostages. It has denied that its military operations constitute genocide.[^cite_note-UN_Commission_of_Inquiry_report_2025_64-65][^cite_note-Casciani_2024a-58][^cite_note-TOI3-11-23-66] Multiple commentators argue that part of the motive is retaliation for the 7 October attacks.[^cite_note-110][^cite_note-Response-111][^cite_note-112] [Nimer Sultany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nimer_Sultany) argues that [anti-Palestinianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Palestinianism) is also a motive.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESultany20242–3-113]

## Definitions of genocide and legal challenges

Main article: [Genocide definitions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_definitions)

The 1948 United Nations [Genocide Convention](143988.md) defines genocide as "*any of the following acts committed with intent to destroy, in whole or in part, a national, ethnical, racial or religious group, as such: killing members of the group; causing serious bodily or mental harm to members of the group; deliberately inflicting on the [group conditions of life calculated to bring about its physical destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_by_attrition)*".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations2014-114][^cite_note-Genocide_Prevention-115] The [International Court of Justice](14918.md) has never held a state liable for genocide.[^cite_note-116][^cite_note-RSIL2024-117] The legal threshold for genocidal intent remains a major barrier to prosecution.[^cite_note-RSIL2024-117]

Scholars have developed broader sociological and cultural definitions that are distinct from the legal definition under the Genocide Convention.[^cite_note-118][^cite_note-119] The public perception of what genocide means is also broader than the legal definition.[^cite_note-120] As to the legal definition, there has been scholarly advocacy for expanding or adjusting it, while others seek to adhere to the original intent under the Genocide Convention.[^cite_note-121][^cite_note-122]

The original definition coined by [Raphael Lemkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raphael_Lemkin) was broader than that used by the Genocide Convention and included [cultural and social destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_genocide). In contrast, orthodox scholarly definitions emphasise actions targeting a group's physical survival.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESemerdjian2024b14-123][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcDoom20243-124] No minimum number of victims[^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNHR202424–25-127] or intended victims[^cite_note-128] is required to establish the crime, nor is complete destruction of the group.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOhlin2009-129] In the [Rohingya genocide case](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rohingya_genocide_case), several states contended that the ICJ should "adopt a balanced approach that recognizes the special gravity of the crime of genocide, without rendering the threshold for inferring genocidal intent so difficult to meet so as to make findings of genocide near-impossible."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJamshidi2024a13-130]

## Genocidal intent and incitement

Main article: [Intent and incitement in the Gaza genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intent_and_incitement_in_the_Gaza_genocide)



Palestinians under the rubble after [Israeli bombing of the Gaza Strip](76139149.md)

Experts affirm that statements by Israeli political and military leaders[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESegalDaniele20241–2-131][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELederman20241–2-132][^cite_note-Asem2025-133]—coupled with [eliminationist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eliminationist) media rhetoric and Israel's conduct in Gaza—indicate [genocidal intent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocidal_intent) and [incitement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incitement_to_genocide) against the [Palestinian people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_people) in Gaza.[^cite_note-Asem2025-133] Israeli officials and journalists[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESegalDaniele20241–2-131][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKulkarni2023-134][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem202567-135] have made verbal statements that [dehumanise Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dehumanization_of_Palestinians_in_Israeli_discourse) and [incite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incitement_to_genocide), [justify](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_justification), or praise atrocities against them as a group.[^cite_note-136][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGessen2024-137] Genocidal intent is also evidenced by the [scale](75130785.md) and systematic nature of actions that exceed any legitimate military objective[^cite_note-138][^cite_note-Asem2025-133]—including the [extensive targeting of children](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intent_and_incitement_in_the_Gaza_genocide#Extensive_targeting_of_children),[^cite_note-UN_Commission_of_Inquiry_report_2025_64-65][^cite_note-Asem2025-133][^cite_note-IAGSResolution-27] widespread sexual violence,[^cite_note-139] [destruction of cultural heritage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destruction_of_cultural_heritage_during_the_Gaza_war),[^cite_note-140][^cite_note-whywecall-141][^cite_note-142] and imposition of [life-destroying conditions](75277791.md)[^cite_note-143][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQuigley2024a-144]—together with the persistence of these practices despite awareness of their catastrophic effects.[^cite_note-145][^cite_note-146]

> "I have ordered a complete siege on the Gaza Strip. There will be no electricity, no food, no fuel, everything is closed. We are fighting human animals, and we are acting accordingly."[^cite_note-147]

— [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant), Minister of Defense of Israel, on 9 October 2023

[^cite_note-148]

[^cite_note-149]

[^cite_note-150]

Both a [United Nations commission of inquiry](69971286.md) and [Amnesty International](18947898.md) documented a "pattern of conduct" by Israeli authorities, concluding that genocidal intent is the "only reasonable inference" that can be drawn from the evidence.[^cite_note-151][^cite_note-152] Other organisations that have attributed genocidal intent to the actions or statements of Israeli officials include a [United Nations panel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_on_the_Exercise_of_the_Inalienable_Rights_of_the_Palestinian_People)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKulkarni2023-134][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECondonCondon2024-153] and [Genocide Watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_Watch).[^cite_note-154][^cite_note-twailr-155] As part of *[Defense for Children International – Palestine v. Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_for_Children_International_%E2%80%93_Palestine_v._Biden)*, the historian [Barry Trachtenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Trachtenberg) said that the rhetoric used by Israeli officials underlies the consensus among genocide historians that the situation in Gaza constitutes genocide.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGessen2024-137] [Navi Pillay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navi_Pillay), the chair of the UN commission of inquiry, compared the statements of Israeli politicians to the genocidal incitement during the [Rwandan genocide](676659.md).[^cite_note-156]

In September 2025, Israeli prime minister Benjamin Netanyahu, president [Isaac Herzog](4645588.md), and former defence minister [Yoav Gallant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoav_Gallant) were found by a United Nations commission of inquiry to have engaged in "direct and public [incitement to commit genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incitement_to_commit_genocide)".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUN_Commission_of_Inquiry_report202564–67-157] Israeli leaders' [repeated references](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intent_and_incitement_in_the_Gaza_genocide#Amalek) to [Amalek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amalek)—the biblical enemy of Israelites whose annihilation is commanded by God—have been considered evidence of genocidal intent by many critics,[^cite_note-158][^cite_note-159] including South Africa.[^cite_note-160][^cite_note-McGreal_2023-161]

## Onset

[B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem), the [South African legal case against Israel](75736183.md), and some scholars who argue Israel is committing genocide argument give 7 October as its start date.[^cite_note-162][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESemerdjian2024a3–4-163][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem202582-164] According to B'Tselem, "The genocidal assault on the residents of Gaza, and on all Palestinians as a group, cannot be understood without acknowledging the impact of the 7 October attack on Israeli society. The shock, fear and humiliation elicited by the attack, and the societal upheaval it triggered, served as a driving force for a shift in government policy toward the Palestinians—from oppression and control to destruction and annihilation."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem20255-88]

During the first 48 hours of Israel's retaliatory attack, IDF chief [Herzi Halevi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herzi_Halevi) reported that the IDF attacked 1,000 targets. According to his wife, he told her that "Gaza will be destroyed". Reportedly, Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) subsequently said he wanted 5,000 targets to be attacked, even though the IDF had not confirmed 5,000 enemy targets. Artificial intelligence was used to generate a list of targets, in many cases based on unconfirmed or outdated intelligence. About 10,000 Palestinians were killed in a month, including entire families. Shmuel Lederman called this "as criminal as it gets".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELederman20257-165] [Martin Shaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Shaw_\(sociologist\)) and [A. Dirk Moses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A._Dirk_Moses) argue that this "front-loaded violence" makes it harder to argue that the genocide began after the initial Israeli attack on Gaza.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw2025b3-166][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMoses202512-167]

On 13 October 2023, the historian [Raz Segal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raz_Segal) said Israel was committing a "textbook case of genocide". He was one of the first scholars to do so.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlein2025-168] Others argue that the war was initially legitimate and the genocide started later, in 2024 or 2025.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw2025b3-166][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMoses202512-167] In September 2024, a UN [Special Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Committee_to_Investigate_Israeli_Practices_Affecting_the_Human_Rights_of_the_Palestinian_People) concluded, "the policies and practices of Israel during the reporting period \[i.e. October 2023 to July 2024] are consistent with the characteristics of genocide."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOHCHR2024d1,_25–26-169]

### Mass killings and physical harm

#### Direct killings

See also: [War crimes in the Gaza war § By Israel](75113830.md#By_Israel), [Gaza humanitarian crisis](75277791.md), and [Casualties of the Gaza war](75130785.md)

UN experts and human rights organisations have characterised Israel's actions in Gaza as [extermination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extermination_\(crime\)),[^cite_note-170] a [crime against humanity](61099.md) that involves "the act of killing on a large scale". During the [first two months of bombing](76139149.md), Israel dropped 25,000 tonnes of explosives on the Gaza Strip. Many of these were unguided bombs dropped in densely populated areas, obliterating entire neighbourhoods.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlbanese2024a6–7-171] Since 7 October 2023, the IDF has been accused of extrajudicial killing of unarmed Palestinians[^cite_note-172][^cite_note-173] and healthcare personnel.[^cite_note-Salman_&_Khadder_2023-174] Israeli soldiers have killed Palestinian civilians, with a December 2023 UN report stating that they allegedly shot them in front of their families.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNHR202437–38-175] In January 2024, Israeli soldiers shot at civilians waving [white flags](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_flag), killing one.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNHR202437–38-175][^cite_note-176] Doctors have identified numerous Palestinian children with single gunshot wounds to the head and chest, consistent with intentional targeting by Israeli forces.[^cite_note-volkskrant-177]

In April 2024, [mass graves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip_mass_graves) were found containing over 300 corpses,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNHR202440-178][^cite_note-un-1148876-179] allegedly including older people, women and wounded,[^cite_note-un-1148876-179] and others with their hands tied.[^cite_note-un-1148876-179] The IDF said accusations that it caused the killings were "baseless and unfounded",[^cite_note-toi-mass-graves-180] and that during its operation "in the area of Nasser Hospital, in accordance to the effort to locate hostages and missing persons, corpses buried by Palestinians in the area of Nasser Hospital were examined."[^cite_note-cnn-mass-graves-181] It added, "Bodies examined, which did not belong to [Israeli hostages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_hostages), were returned to their place."[^cite_note-cnn-mass-graves-181][^cite_note-nytimes-mass-graves-182]

At least 14,000 Palestinians had been killed in Gaza by December 2023.[^cite_note-183] In late 2024, a study limited only to those fatalities confirmed by at least three independent sources, and only from November 2023 to April 2024 (about 8,100 people), corroborated previous reports by the UN and news outlets that 70% of fatalities were women and children.[^cite_note-184][^cite_note-186] As of 31 August 2024, data from the Ministry of Health (only for those of the dead who were fully identified, about 34,000 people at the time) showed that 60% of those killed were women, children, and the elderly.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraham-Harrison2024b-187] Eleven months later (31 July 2025) the number of those of the dead who were fully identified reached 60,199, 52.6% of whom were women, children, and the elderly.[^cite_note-188][^cite_note-189][^cite_note-190]

By 14 January 2024, over 23,900 Gazans had been confirmed killed.[^cite_note-191] By 10 May, deaths had topped 35,000, a third of them unidentified, with over 10,000 more estimated to be buried under the rubble.[^cite_note-KhatibMcKeeYusuf-192] Within the first three weeks, the Israeli assault killed more children in Gaza than were killed worldwide across all conflict zones in any year since 2019.[^cite_note-193][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQutami2023531-194] Over 52,000 people had been wounded by December 2023,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThomas2023-195][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAssociated_Press2023a-196] and by May 2024 this number had risen to over 77,700.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnited_Nations_in_Palestine2024-197][^cite_note-198] As of 10 October 2025, the Gaza Health Ministry reports that at least 67,194 Gazans were killed, which is between 3% and 4% of Gaza's total population.[^cite_note-199]

*[+972 Magazine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/+972_Magazine)* and *Local Call* reported that the IDF decided early in the war to authorise killing up to 15 to 20 civilians per low-ranking militant, while for a senior militant killing more than 100 civilians was authorised. According to six Israeli intelligence officers, a major factor in the high death toll is the military's practice of striking targets in their homes with their families present, chosen partly because automated intelligence systems make those residences easier to identify.[^cite_note-200] Another intelligence officer said that in targeting junior militants, Israel used only [dumb bombs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dumb_bomb), which can destroy entire buildings, to not "waste expensive bombs on unimportant people".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAbraham2024-201]

&#x20;In March 2024, *[Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz)* reported that some Israeli commanders had set up "kill zones" in which soldiers were [commanded to kill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rules_of_engagement) anyone on sight, even if they were unarmed.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKubovich2024a-202][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESultany202411-203] According to an Israeli officer, "in practice, a terrorist is anyone the IDF has killed in the areas in which its forces operate."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKubovich2024a-202][^cite_note-205] In June, the [Associated Press](18935732.md) found that Israel's campaign in Gaza was killing entire bloodlines of Palestinians to a "degree never seen before".[^cite_note-206] According to testimony given to the Israeli [Knesset](74656.md), Israeli soldiers driving [armoured bulldozers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IDF_Caterpillar_D9) had been ordered to "run over terrorists, dead and alive, in the hundreds".[^cite_note-207]



Bags of burned, dismembered limbs, called *[ashlaa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashlaa)* (أشلاء) in Arabic,

[^cite_note-208]

[^cite_note-209]

&#x20;from the 10 August 2024 [Israeli airstrike on the Al-Tabaeen School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Tabaeen_school_attack), which was sheltering [displaced](427408.md) Palestinians

The proportion of women and children among the dead is disputed,[^cite_note-Horton_et_al.-210][^cite_note-211] but the names, gender, and age of 60,000+ of the victims are known and published.[^cite_note-techforpalestine-kills-212] On 7 May 2024, total deaths quoted by the UN were 34,735, of which 24,686 are fully identified: 52% women and children, 8% elderly of all genders, and 40% men.[^cite_note-Horton_et_al.-210] In November 2024, the UN published an analysis covering 8,119 fatalities that were verified by at least three independent sources, between November 2023 and April 2024, with 70% of them women and children.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESaric2024-213] As of 31 August 2024, per the Gaza Ministry of Health, the number of fatalities had risen to 40,691, 34,344 identified by name: 17,652 (51%) women and children, 2,955 (9%) elderly of all genders (defined as those aged 60 or older), and 13,737 (40%) men.[^cite_note-214][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraham-Harrison2024b-187][^cite_note-215] A year later (31 July 2025), per the Gaza Ministry of Health, the number of fatalities identified by name reached 60,199: 28,728 (48%) were women and children, 2,928 (5%) were elderly of all genders (defined as those aged 65 or older), and 28,543 (47%) were men. A minimum of 18,457 children had been killed since October 2023.[^cite_note-techforpalestine-kills-212]

In November 2024, the Gaza Health Ministry reported that 1,410 Gazan families had been completely erased from the civil registry as a result of Israeli bombings.[^cite_note-216] Data collection has become increasingly difficult for the Gaza Health Ministry due to the destruction of infrastructure.[^cite_note-KhatibMcKeeYusuf-192] The ministry has had to supplement its usual reporting based on hospital dead with other sources of information,[^cite_note-KhatibMcKeeYusuf-192] including reports by the media and first responders as well as families and widows, who must formally register their husbands' deaths to qualify for government assistance.[^cite_note-217] Professor [Michael Spagat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Spagat) found an urgent need for a transparent methodology to reconcile its top-line death numbers—34,535 as of 30 April 2024—with its detailed breakdowns, including only those of the dead who were fully identified, summing to 24,653 on the same date.[^cite_note-Spagat_2024a-39] By August 2025, this gap between the number of victims and the number of fully identified vicitms became smaller (about 62,000 vs. about 60,000), as more victims were identified.[^cite_note-218] The ministry's figures for the total number killed have been contested by Israeli authorities but accepted as accurate by Israeli intelligence services, the UN, and the [World Health Organization](33583.md).[^cite_note-KhatibMcKeeYusuf-192] More recently, *The Guardian* has reported that a classified IDF database registered 8,900 killed militants. This and GHM's data imply a civilian death rate of 83%, which exceeds that of any conflict since 1989 except the [Rwandan genocide](676659.md), the [Siege of Mariupol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Mariupol), and the [Siege of Srebrenica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Srebrenica).[^cite_note-219] The IDF denied this, saying the figures were inaccurate and inconsistent with its data.[^cite_note-220]

A 2025 paper on the Gaza war estimated 64,260 deaths from traumatic injury between October 2023 and 30 June 2024, and likely exceeding 70,000 by October 2024, with 59.1% being women, children and the elderly. It concluded that the GHM undercounted trauma-related deaths by 41% in its report, and also noted that its findings "underestimate the full impact of the military operation in Gaza, as they do not account for non-trauma-related deaths resulting from health service disruption, food insecurity, and inadequate water and sanitation."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJamaluddineAbukmailAlyCampbell2025-221] A comparable estimate for traumatic injury deaths was around 80,000 for January 2025.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBloxham202523-222] A February study in *[The Lancet](463899.md)* estimated that life expectancy in the Gaza Strip between October 2023 and September 2024 decreased by 34.9 years, excluding indirect deaths. The study also used census and registration data to assess the reliability of the Gaza Health Ministry's death count, and found no substantial errors.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuillotDraidiCetorelliMonteiro_Da_Silva2025478–485-223]

In May 2025, finance minister [Bezalel Smotrich](46196834.md) claimed that Israel was targeting Hamas's civilian workers, saying, "We're eliminating ministers, bureaucrats, money handlers—everyone who holds up Hamas's civilian rule."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShpigel2025-224] Killing civilian members of Hamas is in itself illegal.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJamshidi2024b-225] Starting in June 2025, IDF soldiers said they were ordered to [shoot at](80075155.md) crowds of Palestinians near [Gaza Humanitarian Foundation](79923808.md) aid sites,[^cite_note-226] killing over 1,000 people.[^cite_note-227] Amnesty International alleged Israel was trying to restrict aid to starve and inflict genocide upon the Palestinians.[^cite_note-228] In November 2025, a study by the [Max Planck Institute for Demographic Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Planck_Institute_for_Demographic_Research) estimated that the total number of violent deaths in Gaza was between 100,000 and 126,000, of which 27% were children under 15 years old, and 24% women.[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231] Amnesty International and B'Tselem asserted the genocide continued during the ceasefire, partly citing the continued killing of Palestinians.[^cite_note-Alj-232] Between the start of the ceasefire in October 2025 and June 2026, Israel killed over 1,000 people,[^cite_note-233] including at least 265 children.[^cite_note-234]

According to a June 2026 UN commission report, Israel deliberately killed Palestinian children during the ‌war and after the October 2025 ceasefire, making up around 30% of those killed in Gaza overall, and this was a key element in establishing Israel's genocidal intent in Gaza.[^cite_note-Holmes-21][^cite_note-ohchrjune26-23]

According to an Al Jazeera Arabic investigation based on witness testimonies and expert analysis, there are nearly 3,000 Palestinians dead without recoverable remains, believed to be connected to Israeli forces' use of US-supplied thermal and [thermobaric munitions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thermobaric_weapon) in Gaza.[^cite_note-235]

#### Indirect deaths



Mother cries for her 4-year-old daughter, who died due to [malnutrition](258979.md) and lack of treatment

Rasha Khatib, [Martin McKee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_McKee), and [Salim Yusuf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salim_Yusuf) published an estimate of the number of deaths, directly or indirectly caused by the conflict, that by July 2024 had already occurred or would occur in the coming months and years. Indirect Palestinian deaths from disease are expected to be much higher due to the intensity of the conflict, destruction of healthcare infrastructure, lack of food, water, shelter, and safe places for civilians to flee, and reduction in [UNRWA](32270.md) funding. They estimated that the total conflict-related deaths in Gaza will likely be four to 16 times higher than the reported death toll. By multiplying the reported deaths by five, they argued that "186,000 or even more deaths could be attributable to the current conflict in Gaza".[^cite_note-KhatibMcKeeYusuf-192] Spagat wrote that their estimate "lacks a solid foundation and is implausible",[^cite_note-236][^cite_note-Bigg_2024-237] but it was "fair to call attention to the fact that not all of the deaths are going to be direct violent ones", and has called the death toll in Gaza "staggeringly high".[^cite_note-Horton_et_al.-210][^cite_note-Bigg_2024-237] [Donald Bloxham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Donald_Bloxham) also notes that most deaths have been "indirect deaths" in various wars and that the "systematic obstruction of supplies into Gaza" is an Israeli policy, which makes calling these deaths "indirect" incorrect.[^cite_note-Bloxham_2025-238]

An October 2024 letter by US healthcare workers who had served in Gaza since 7 October 2023 tried to estimate the number of Gazans who had died of starvation based on publicly available IPC reports.[^cite_note-239] It said the most conservative estimate was that at least 62,413 people in Gaza had died from starvation, most of them young children; this estimate was based on the assumption that catastrophic (level 5) food insecurity results in a death rate of at least 2 deaths per 10,000 people per day.[^cite_note-240] The physicians also estimated that at least 5,000 people had died from lack of access to care for chronic diseases.[^cite_note-241][^cite_note-WatsonInstituteEstimatedDeaths-242][^cite_note-GazaHealthcareLettersEstimatedDeaths-243] The indirect death estimates in two studies reviewed by *The Economist* implied that the life expectancy in Gaza has fallen by 35 years, rivalling the [Rwandan genocide](676659.md) in absolute terms.[^cite_note-244][^cite_note-245]

#### Serious bodily and mental harm

See also: [Effect of the Gaza war on children in the Gaza Strip](75987631.md) and [Women in the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_the_Gaza_war)





**Top to bottom**:&#x20;

- Yamen was injured in his leg during his sixth displacement in Jabalia refugee camp, and he was forced to flee while wounded.

- A Palestinian refugee carries his injured grandchildren from the Israeli bombing of Nuseirat Camp, Gaza Strip

The number of injured since 7 October 2023 due to Israeli military actions is greater than 174,000,[^cite_note-snapshot-6][^cite_note-Burke_2025-19] and Gaza has the most child amputees per capita worldwide.[^cite_note-Amputee_stats-45] In the same period over 1,040 Palestinians have been killed in the West Bank.[^cite_note-246]

By March 2024, an estimated 17,000 children were "[wounded child, no surviving family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wounded_child,_no_surviving_family)" (WCNSF), a new medical term.[^cite_note-247][^cite_note-volkskrant-177] According to a UN committee, the Gaza war has resulted in disabilities for more than 21,000 children as of September 2025.[^cite_note-Guardian_3_September_2025-46]

As of 25 August 2024, the UN estimates that most of Gaza's 2.2 million people are confined to roughly 15 square miles (39 km<sup>2</sup>), causing a critical lack of basic services, like clean water, and diseases spreading widely, such as [Hepatitis C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hepatitis_C).[^cite_note-248]

#### Torture and sexual violence

Main articles: [Sexual and gender-based violence against Palestinians during the Gaza war](76149089.md) and [Torture during the Gaza war](76400472.md)

Israel has been accused of indiscriminate [mass detentions](75234504.md)[^cite_note-249][^cite_note-250] while prisoners said they were threatened with mutilation,[^cite_note-251] death, arson, and rape,[^cite_note-252] and torturing Palestinians detained without charges.[^cite_note-253]

In August 2024, the UN OHCHR reported receiving testimony from Palestinians imprisoned at [Sde Teiman detention camp](76887648.md) about rape and sexual assault perpetrated on detainees.[^cite_note-ohchr-2024-08-4] The Lemkin Institute considers this and similar reports to be indicative of "Sexualized Violence During Genocide", or sexual violence being used to destroy a group.[^cite_note-lemkin-254]

Amnesty International reported that the pattern of abuses inside Israeli prisons "underscores the systematic dehumanization and mental and physical abuse of Palestinians in Gaza and may also be taken into account with a view to inferring genocidal intent from pattern of conduct."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAmnesty_International_report2024296-255] According to the [Independent International Commission of Inquiry](69971286.md), gender-based and sexual violence were committed "to dominate, oppress and destroy the Palestinian people in whole or in part."[^cite_note-Independent_International_Commission_Inquiry_2025_sex_vio-256]

A report by UN Special Rapporteur [Francesca Albanese](72566699.md) called Israel's torture of Palestinians "a structural feature of the ongoing genocide and broader settler-colonial apartheid." According to Albanese, torture occurs both in Israeli prisons and in the entirety of occupied Palestine through "mass displacement, siege, denial of aid and food, unrestrained military and settler violence and pervasive surveillance and terror".[^cite_note-257]

### Starvation, displacement, and destruction of living conditions

#### Starvation and blockade

Main articles: [Gaza Strip famine](75828824.md) and [Blockade of the Gaza Strip](19224310.md)







**Clockwise from top**:&#x20;

- A four-year-old Palestinian girl who died due to [malnutrition](258979.md) and lack of treatment

- Displaced Palestinians gather to receive food from a charity in Deir el-Balah, Gaza Strip

- Displaced Palestinians receive food from charitable Tekiya during Ramadan in Deir el-Balah, Gaza Strip

In February 2024, [Human Rights Watch](46530.md) and [Amnesty International](18947898.md) both released statements declaring Israel had failed to comply with the ICJ's 26 January ruling to prevent genocide by [blocking aid](75013719.md) from entering Gaza.[^cite_note-Human_Rights_Watch_Blockade-62][^cite_note-Amnesty_International_Blockade-61][^cite_note-258] A Refugees International report found that Israel had "consistently and groundlessly impeded aid operations within Gaza".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJohnson2024b-259] The historian Melanie Tanielian argues that starvation and blockade should be foregrounded as methods of genocide alongside mass bombing.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETanielian20243–4-260] In an April report, [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem) called the unfolding famine "the product of a deliberate and conscious Israeli policy".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESultany20247-261][^cite_note-262] [Elyse Semerdjian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elyse_Semerdjian) characterised Israel's actions as [genocide by attrition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_by_attrition).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESemerdjian2024b-263]

In October 2023, the [World Food Programme](33582.md) warned of Gaza's dwindling food supply,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETanielian20246–7-264] and in December, alongside the UN, it reported that more than half of Gaza's population was "starving", fewer than one in ten were eating every day, and 48% were suffering "extreme hunger".[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267] Palestinian Foreign Minister [Riyad al-Maliki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riyad_al-Maliki) referred to "Israel's deliberate use of starvation as a weapon of war against the people it occupied"; an Israeli official called the charge "blood-libellous" and "delusional".[^cite_note-268] In December 2023, Human Rights Watch found that Israel was using starvation as a weapon of war by deliberately denying access to food and water.[^cite_note-269] In January 2024, UN experts accused Israel of "destroying Gaza's food system and using food as a weapon against the Palestinian people".[^cite_note-270] In February 2024, finance minister Bezalel Smotrich personally blocked US-funded shipments of flour from entering Gaza, in violation of promises Israel had made to the US government.[^cite_note-271]

&#x20;In early 2024, the [United Nations special rapporteur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_special_rapporteur) on the right to food [Michael Fakhri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Fakhri) said that Israel is "culpable" of genocide because "Israel has announced its intention to destroy the Palestinian people, in whole or in part, simply for being Palestinian" and because Israel was denying food to Palestinians by halting humanitarian aid and "intentionally" destroying&#x20;

> small-scale fishing vessels, greenhouses and orchards in Gaza ... We have never seen a civilian population made to go so hungry so quickly and so completely, that is the consensus among starvation experts. Israel is not just targeting civilians, it is trying to damn the future of the Palestinian people by harming their children.[^cite_note-272]

&#x20;After the ICJ ruling, the number of aid trucks Israel allowed into Gaza dropped by 40%.[^cite_note-273] In the ICJ's March reaffirmation of provisional measures, the court highlighted the "unprecedented levels of food insecurity experienced by Palestinians in the Gaza Strip over recent weeks, as well as the increasing risks of epidemics",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESultany20245-274] acknowledging that since the Court's January order there had been a "lack of Israeli compliance" resulting in "the catastrophic living conditions" deteriorating further.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESultany20246-275]

In March 2024, 12 Israeli human rights organisations signed an open letter accusing Israel of failing to abide by the ICJ ruling to prevent genocide by facilitating the delivery of humanitarian aid.[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJohnson2024a-277] In April the UN special rapporteur on the right to health [Tlaleng Mofokeng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tlaleng_Mofokeng) said Israel was "killing and causing irreparable harm against Palestinian civilians with its bombardments", adding, "They are also knowingly and intentionally imposing [famine](75828824.md)" and accusing Israel of "genocide".[^cite_note-Al-Monitor-2024-04-22-278]

In October 2024, Israel had reportedly adopted a modified version of the [Generals' Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Generals'_Plan).[^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280][^cite_note-282] The proposed plan included orders for all residents of northern Gaza to leave within a week; a full siege on water, food, and fuel; and then the arrest or killing of all who remained.[^cite_note-283][^cite_note-284] By mid-October 2024, Israel had ordered the evacuation of northern Gaza and prevented the entry of humanitarian aid for almost two weeks.[^cite_note-285][^cite_note-286] According to Stephen Devereux, avoidable deaths due to starvation as a result of Israeli policies "almost certainly constitutes a war crime and a crime against humanity".[^cite_note-287]

On 21 November 2024, the International Criminal Court [issued arrest warrants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ICC_arrest_warrants_for_Israeli_leaders) for Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu and the former defence minister Yoav Gallant, asserting that the two "bear criminal responsibility for the war crime of starvation as a method of warfare".[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-289][^cite_note-El_Abdallah_2024-290]

Israel lifted restrictions on aid into Gaza in January–February 2025 during the first stage of [the January ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2025_Gaza_war_ceasefire). But on 2 March, Israel announced that all humanitarian aid would be blocked indefinitely unless Hamas agreed to alter the terms of the ceasefire deal, which Hamas refused to do.[^cite_note-291][^cite_note-292] Within four days, food supplies in Gaza had rapidly depleted while the price of food had more than doubled. Aid agencies such as [Oxfam](19698439.md) and [UNICEF](19698110.md) warned of mass starvation if the aid freeze continued. Oxfam policy lead Bushra Khalidi predicted "the total collapse of systems that sustain life".[^cite_note-293] Lawyer Salah Abdel-Ati said Israel's actions were illegal under the [Geneva Conventions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva_Conventions), which prohibits the destruction or withholding of essentials such as food in combat zones.[^cite_note-294]

In May 2025, after blocking the import of all food, medicine, and fuel for two and a half months,[^cite_note-295] Netanyahu announced that Israel would allow "minimal humanitarian aid" into Gaza due to international pressure.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShpigel2025-224] Israel has proposed using private companies to distribute aid to the south of Gaza only. The plan is backed by the US, which has created the [Gaza Humanitarian Foundation](79923808.md) (GHF) to deliver aid without "Hamas stealing, looting or leveraging this assistance for its own ends". The United Nations aid chief [Tom Fletcher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Fletcher_\(diplomat\)) criticised the plan, saying it "forces further displacement" and "makes aid conditional on political and military aims".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENichols2025a-296] Numerous Palestinians have been [killed by Israeli forces](80075155.md) while approaching GHF aid distribution points.[^cite_note-297]

In August 2025, it was reported that Israel planned to surge aid to other parts of Gaza while cutting off all aid to Gaza City to force residents to evacuate while Israel takes over the city.[^cite_note-298] As of August 2025, projections show the entire population is experiencing "high levels of acute food insecurity", with about 641,000 people experiencing [catastrophic levels](16431152.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIPC_Report_August20251-48] The IPC confirmed famine is taking place in the [Gaza Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Governorate).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEIPC_Report_August20251-48][^cite_note-299]

Two weeks after the [October 2025 ceasefire](81215079.md) came into effect, dozens of NGOs, including the [Norwegian Refugee Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norwegian_Refugee_Council) and [Doctors Without Borders](20498.md), announced that Israel was arbitrarily blocking their shipments of aid to Gaza. World Health Organisation head Tedros Adhanom Ghebreyesus said, "the situation still remains catastrophic because what's entering is not enough" and "there is no dent in hunger because there is not enough food".[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301]

By December 2025, the food situation improved, with famine and catastrophic food insecurity (level 5) no longer happening; but 27% of the population still experienced emergency-level food insecurity (level 4) and another 50% of the population experienced crisis-level food insecurity (level 3).[^cite_note-302][^cite_note-303] At the turn of the year, Israel's expulsion of Doctors Without Borders and several other humanitarian organizations from Gaza raised fears that the effects of malnutrition on the population would not be adequately treated or monitored; these organizations helped alleviate effects of famine/malnutrition by treating children with severe acute malnutrition.[^cite_note-304][^cite_note-305] Israel has also continued blocking certain medical aid.[^cite_note-Alj-232]

After the outbreak of the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md) in February, Israel reimposed several restrictions on Gaza. It closed multiple aid crossings and suspended United Nations humanitarian movements and rotations, the return of people to Gaza, and medical evacuations.[^cite_note-306][^cite_note-307]

#### Forced displacement

See also: [Gaza Strip evacuations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip_evacuations)







**Clockwise from top**:&#x20;

- Return of displaced people via Al-Rasheed Street after ceasefire, January 2025, Gaza Strip

- An aerial photo of displaced Palestinians waiting in northern Nuseirat to return to their homes in Gaza

- An aerial view of Al-Mawasi area where displaced Palestinians live in tents, Gaza Strip

Since October 2023, Israel's evacuation orders have displaced 1.9 million people—nearly Gaza's entire population—through unclear, inconsistent directives, often issued amid bombings, leaving civilians with no safe routes or destinations. Humanitarian zones were repeatedly attacked, while Israel blocked aid and destroyed infrastructure, leading to starvation and uninhabitable conditions. Senior Israeli officials openly declared intentions to reduce Gaza's territory and push Palestinians out, reinforcing policies of [ethnic cleansing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_cleansing) and permanent displacement. Human Rights Watch reported that Israel's systematic forced displacement of Palestinians in Gaza amounts to [war crimes](48626.md) and [crimes against humanity](61099.md).[^cite_note-308][^cite_note-309][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGómez-UgarteChenAcostaBasellini202513-44] South Africa and others criticised the [Gaza Strip evacuations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Strip_evacuations) as a key component of the genocide.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJamshidi2024a14–16-310] B'Tselem mentions statements by Israeli high-ranking officials that a "central objective of the war" was ethnic cleansing.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem202547-311]

On 6 October 2024, Israel designated northern Gaza as a combat zone and ordered the civilian population to evacuate.[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313] Both Israeli military analysts and the [Al Mezan Center for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Mezan_Center_for_Human_Rights) alleged that this was the first stage of the "[Generals' Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_generals'_plan)", a policy proposed by the former Israeli general [Giora Eiland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giora_Eiland) to force Palestinians out of Gaza.[^cite_note-314] The [UN Human Rights Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Human_Rights_Office) said that Israel may have caused the "destruction of the Palestinian population in Gaza's northernmost governorate through death and displacement."[^cite_note-315]

During the [January 2025 ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2025_Gaza_war_ceasefire), displaced persons were able to return to their homes in northern Gaza. After Israel [broke the ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_2025_Israeli_attacks_on_the_Gaza_Strip) in March 2025, displacement orders resumed. The [2025 Gaza City offensive](80801475.md) led to further evacuation orders for 1.2 million people.[^cite_note-316] From the start of the war to the end of 2025, about 100,000 people fled the Gaza Strip. Combined with deaths, this resulted in a 6% decline in the territory's population.[^cite_note-317]

#### Ecocide

Main article: [Environmental impact of the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_impact_of_the_Gaza_war)



Waste has accumulated near populated areas across Gaza, posing environmental and health risks.

Israel's bombing in Gaza caused [severe environmental devastation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_impact_of_the_Gaza_war), destroying vegetation, water, sanitation, and waste infrastructure and contaminating air, soil, and water. The UN and environmental groups report damage consistent with "[ecocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecocide)", citing massive debris, untreated sewage, and toxic pollution. These conditions have triggered disease outbreaks, respiratory illnesses, and long-term health risks.[^cite_note-318][^cite_note-Ahmed_et_al.-5][^cite_note-319] Multiple commentators have argued that deliberate ecocide is a central component of Israel's genocide in Gaza.[^cite_note-320][^cite_note-321][^cite_note-322] Scholars have argued that the destruction of the environment sustaining a population—including its water, land, and food systems—constitutes a genocidal act, as it inflicts conditions of life calculated to destroy the group, in whole or in part.[^cite_note-Article2GC-324][^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326]

#### Deliberate destruction of civilian infrastructure

See also: [Israeli bombing of the Gaza Strip](76139149.md)







**Clockwise from top**:&#x20;

- An aerial view showing destruction in Rafah after Israeli forces withdrawal and as the ceasefire took hold, Gaza Strip

- Damage following an Israeli airstrike on the El-Remal aera in Gaza City on 9 October 2023.

- Smoke and flames billow after Israeli forces struck a high-rise tower in Gaza City

[Mark Levene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Levene) and [Elyse Semerdjian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elyse_Semerdjian) locate the mass destruction of infrastructure within Israel's [Dahiya doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dahiya_doctrine), implemented against Gaza since 2006, with Levene calling it [urbicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urbicide) and a tool of genocide.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELevene20246–7-327][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESemerdjian2024a2-328] In October 2024, [Forensic Architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forensic_Architecture) concluded, "Israel's military campaign in Gaza is organised, systematic, and intended to destroy conditions of life and life-sustaining infrastructure".[^cite_note-329]

In July 2025, *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* reported that "about 70% of the structures in Gaza are either completely destroyed or severely damaged". Israel was reportedly paying contractors up to 5,000 shekels per building demolished.[^cite_note-330] In February 2025 it was reported that at least 15 children had died of [hypothermia](146879.md) over the winter due to Israel's destruction of housing and power facilities.[^cite_note-331] In May 2025, Netanyahu said, "we are destroying more and more homes, and Gazans have nowhere to return to. The only inevitable outcome will be the wish of Gazans to emigrate outside of the Gaza Strip."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem202547-311]

In September 2026, updated satellite imagery on [Google Maps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Google_Maps) offered a comprehensive view of the destruction wrought across the Gaza Strip by the Israeli military campaign. The aerial images showed that entire residential neighborhoods had vanished, while vast urban areas had been reduced to fields of rubble and debris. The updated imagery captured a striking transformation of Gaza's urban landscape, in which destruction extended beyond individual buildings to encompass whole residential blocks, leaving large expanses of empty land where densely populated neighborhoods once stood. By allowing comparisons between Gaza's present urban landscape and its pre-war condition, the images provided a broader perspective on the scale of the damage across neighborhoods and residential blocks.[^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333]

In a December 2024 report, Human Rights Watch accused Israel of committing acts of genocide in Gaza by targeting water and sanitation infrastructure and depriving Palestinians of adequate access to water. The report alleges that Israel intentionally damaged solar panels powering treatment plants, a reservoir, and warehouses, while blocking repair materials and fuel for generators, cutting electricity supplies, and attacking workers.[^cite_note-hrw-report-334][^cite_note-Berg2024-335] According to B'Tselem, Israel has destroyed 84% of Gaza's water facilities,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem202535-336] while in a report authored and published by the [World Bank](36752.md), [European Union](9317.md), and United Nations it was reported that 89% of Gaza's water, sanitation, and hygiene facilities had been destroyed or damaged.[^cite_note-337] UN Special Rapporteur [Pedro Arrojo-Agudo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedro_Arrojo-Agudo) called these attacks "an important part of a genocidal strategy".[^cite_note-338]

Rafah, a city in the southern Gaza Strip, has been razed;[^cite_note-339] this has been mostly carried out by bulldozing and controlled demolitions of buildings, rather than by aerial bombardment.[^cite_note-nytrafah-340] In July 2025, the BBC reported that Israel had engaged in controlled demolitions of civilian infrastructure, potentially in violation of the Geneva Convention.[^cite_note-BBCVerifyDemolitions-341] The BBC reported an IDF spokesperson saying, "Hamas and other terrorist organizations conceal military assets in densely populated civilian areas. The IDF identifies and destroys terrorist infrastructure located, among other places, within buildings in these areas."[^cite_note-BBCVerifyDemolitions-341] In November 2025, the BBC reported that Israel had demolished over 1,500 more buildings in Gaza since the ceasefire with Hamas.[^cite_note-BBCCeasfireDemolitions-342] A spokesperson for the IDF said it was acting "in accordance with the ceasefire framework" as the demolitions occurred behind the [Yellow Line](81476537.md), in the portion of Gaza under Israeli military control, which comprises 53% of Gaza.[^cite_note-BBCCeasfireDemolitions-342]

#### Preventing births

In March 2025, a UN investigation concluded that Israel had committed genocidal acts in Gaza by systematically destroying its reproductive healthcare facilities while imposing a siege preventing necessary medications for deliveries, pregnancies, and [neonatal care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neonatal_care), causing "irreversible" harm to Palestinians' reproductive prospects in Gaza. The commission also found that Israeli forces intentionally destroyed Gaza's main [in-vitro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IVF) [fertility clinic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fertility_clinic), Al-Basma IVF Centre, which served 2,000 to 3,000 patients monthly. Israel destroyed about 4,000 [embryos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embryo) and 1,000 specimens of [sperm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sperm) and unfertilised [eggs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egg_cell) in the attack.[^cite_note-343][^cite_note-344] No evidence was found that the building was used for military purposes. The commission concluded that the clinic's destruction "was a measure intended to prevent births among Palestinians in Gaza, which is a genocidal act".[^cite_note-Independent_International_Commission_Inquiry_2025_births-345][^cite_note-NDTV-March_13-346][^cite_note-Le_Poidevin_2025a-347] UN experts reported that they had found that Israel had systematically destroyed women's health care facilities and used sexual violence as a war strategy, thereby carrying out genocidal acts against Palestinians.[^cite_note-NDTV-March_13-346][^cite_note-Le_Poidevin_2025a-347]

According to statistics from the [Gaza Ministry of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_Ministry_of_Health) reported by [Physicians for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physicians_for_Human_Rights), the birth rate in Gaza in May and June 2025 had fallen 41% from the same months in 2022. The ministry also reported an increase in infant mortality, miscarriages, and premature births in Gaza during this time.[^cite_note-348]

### Destruction of civilian and social systems

#### Attacks on healthcare

Main article: [Attacks on health facilities during the Gaza war](75314000.md)





**Top to bottom**:&#x20;

- Destruction of UNRWA [el-Sheikh Radwan](21912219.md) health centre, February 2024

- Palestinian Red Crescent Personnel inspect a destroyed ambulance in Deir el-Balah, Gaza Strip

In November 2023 in *[The Lancet](463899.md)* and in February 2024 in *[BMJ Global Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMJ_Global_Health)*, multiple doctors detailed how the targeting of Gazan health infrastructure and medical personnel, coupled with rhetoric used by Israeli politicians, amounts to genocide.[^cite_note-349] Legal scholars have supported this assessment.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMahomed202377–79-350][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESultany202411-203] [Gaza's healthcare system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_the_State_of_Palestine) faced humanitarian crises as a result of Israel's assault: hospitals began shutting down by 23 October as they ran out of fuel.[^cite_note-351] When hospitals lost power, multiple [premature babies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023%E2%80%932024_Gaza_Strip_preterm_births) in [NICUs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neonatal_intensive_care_unit) died.[^cite_note-352][^cite_note-353][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESoni202380-354]

Israeli airstrikes have killed numerous medical staffers, and ambulances and health institutions have been destroyed.[^cite_note-Healthcare_destroyed-355] *[Médecins Sans Frontières](20498.md)* reported that scores of ambulances and medical facilities were damaged or destroyed,[^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357] and that its own staff were killed.[^cite_note-358][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKhanTinua2024805–806-359] The [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md) said the healthcare system had "totally collapsed".[^cite_note-360] In April 2024, UN special rapporteur on the right to health [Tlaleng Mofokeng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tlaleng_Mofokeng) said, "The destruction of healthcare facilities continues to catapult to proportions yet to be fully quantified."[^cite_note-Al-Monitor-2024-04-22-278]

As of February 2025, at least 160 healthcare workers from Gaza are believed to be detained by Israel, with another 24 missing after being taken from Gaza hospitals. Al-Shifa hospital director [Mohammed Abu Salmiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammed_Abu_Salmiya), detained for seven months and released without charges, detailed the abuses he faced and said that in Israeli prisons "no day passes without torture".[^cite_note-361]

#### Destruction of cultural, religious and educational sites

Main article: [Destruction of cultural heritage during the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Destruction_of_cultural_heritage_during_the_Gaza_war)

See also: [Attacks on schools during the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attacks_on_schools_during_the_Gaza_war), [Israeli razing of cemeteries and necroviolence against Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_razing_of_cemeteries_and_necroviolence_against_Palestinians), and [Attacks on religious sites during the Israeli invasion of Gaza](77727480.md)







**Clockwise from top**:&#x20;

- A mosque in Gaza destroyed in an Israeli airstrike, 20 February 2025.

- The wreckage of the [Rashad Shawa Cultural Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashad_Shawa_Cultural_Center)

- A mosque in Khan Younis, 8 October 2023

[Amnesty International](18947898.md) notes that "while the destruction of historical, cultural and religious property or heritage is not considered a prohibited act under the Genocide Convention, the ICJ has established that such destruction can provide evidence of intent to physically destroy the group when carried out deliberately."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAmnesty_International_report202432-362] The [Lemkin Institute for Genocide Prevention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lemkin_Institute_for_Genocide_Prevention) has said that Israel's deliberate destruction of cemeteries in Gaza indicates genocidal intent because it enacts the "[erasure of a people's...historical presence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_negationism)".[^cite_note-whywecall-141]

Since 7 October 2023, the IDF has been accused of using excessive force against dozens of schools;[^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364] theft;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKottasováSalmanAlkhaldiBashir2023-365] desecration and mutilation of deceased Palestinians;[^cite_note-Salman_&_Khadder_2023-174] and making no, or an inadequate, distinction between Hamas forces and civilians.[^cite_note-366] The targeting of cultural and educational sites have also been cited as genocidal acts, as has the use of [white phosphorus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_phosphorus).[^cite_note-368][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEÜngör20248-369]

On 18 April 2024, UN experts in [Geneva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geneva) condemned Israel for its "[scholasticide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scholasticide)" in Gaza, finding that it had destroyed more than 80% of schools and killed 5,000 students, 261 teachers, and dozens of professors.[^cite_note-scholasticide-52]

Amnesty International identified at least four instances in which there was "no imperative military necessity" for the deliberate destruction of Gazan cultural and religious sites:[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAmnesty_International_report2024216-370] the destruction of the Al-Mughraqa campus of [Al-Azhar University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Azhar_University_%E2%80%93_Gaza), the Al-Zahra campus of [Israa University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israa_University), the Al-Dhilal mosque and Bani Suheila cemetery in Khan Younis, and the Al-Istiqlal mosque in Khan Younis.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAmnesty_International_report2024222–233-371] Amnesty International pointed to the attitudes and behaviour of Israeli soldiers involved in the demolitions of these sites in videos posted on social media as evidence that these actions demonstrated genocidal intent. Amnesty International also noted the overall volume of destruction of Gazan cultural, historical and religious sites, including Gaza's central archives.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAmnesty_International_report2024216–219-372]

As of January 2025, Israel had destroyed 815 mosques and 19 cemeteries during the Gaza war.[^cite_note-373]

In June 2025, UN experts published a report saying Israel had committed the crime against humanity of [extermination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extermination_\(crime\)) for "killing civilians sheltering in schools and religious sites". According to the report, Israel has destroyed over 90% of educational buildings in Gaza.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMurphy2025-374]

### Targeting of specific groups

#### Educators

The killing of educators and destruction of educational infrastructure such as schools and universities has been recognised by scholars as a component of the Gaza genocide.[^cite_note-375][^cite_note-376] In support of its 2025 resolution declaring that Israel's policies and actions in Gaza had met the legal definition of genocide, the IAGS wrote, "Israel has destroyed schools, universities, libraries, museums, and archives, all of them essential to the continued existence of Palestinian collective well-being and identity".[^cite_note-IAGSResolution-27]

[United Nations special rapporteurs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_special_rapporteur) and experts suggested there was "an intentional effort to comprehensively destroy the Palestinian education system, an action known as '[scholasticide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scholasticide)'". They said the attacks "present a systematic pattern of violence aimed at dismantling the very foundation of Palestinian society".[^cite_note-scholasticide-52]

In its filing with the ICJ charging Israel with the crime of genocide, South Africa cited the destruction of four universities and the killing of leading Palestinian academics Professor [Sufian Tayeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufian_Tayeh), president of the Islamic University; Ahmed Hamdi Abo Absa, dean of the software engineering department at the University of Palestine; Muhammad Eid Shabir, professor of immunology and virology and former president of the Islamic University of Gaza; and [Refaat Alareer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refaat_Alareer), poet and professor of comparative literature and creative writing at the Islamic University of Gaza.[^cite_note-ICJ-2023-12-29-55]

Researchers at Birzeit university called the killing of educators "deliberate and systematic", saying that "94 professors were targeted and killed by Israeli occupation".[^cite_note-377]

#### Journalists



"Language makes genocide justifiable. A reason why we are still being bombed after 243 days is because of *The New York Times* and most Western media." – [Hossam Shabat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hossam_Shabat), a Palestinian journalist whom Israel [accused without evidence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_public_diplomacy_in_the_Gaza_war#Discrediting_criticism_by_associating_it_with_Hamas)

[^cite_note-378]

&#x20;of being a member of Hamas and [killed](75146557.md) in an [airstrike](76139149.md) 24 March 2025

[^cite_note-379]

Press freedom organisations and human rights groups have characterised the systematic killing of Palestinian journalists as a component of the genocide.[^cite_note-380] [The International Federation of Journalists](1178863.md) reported that 56 media professionals were killed in 2025 alone as part of the "genocidal war on Gaza".[^cite_note-381]

South Africa cited Palestinian journalists being "killed at a rate significantly higher than has occurred in any conflict in the past 100 years" as part of its [application accusing Israel](75736183.md) of genocide.[^cite_note-ICJ-2023-12-29-55]

The IDF has been accused of directly targeting journalists and media organisations despite clear marking and protective measures. Al Jazeera reported that 278 journalists and 706 family members of Palestinian journalists have been killed during the "genocidal war" since October 2023.[^cite_note-382][^cite_note-Akila-383]

A [Brown University](4157.md) study found that Israel had killed more journalists in Gaza since October 2023 than "the U.S. Civil War, World Wars I and II, the Korean War, Vietnam War, the wars in Yugoslavia in the 1990s and 2000s, and the post-9/11 war in Afghanistan, combined".[^cite_note-384][^cite_note-385]

The [CPJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Committee_to_Protect_Journalists) observed that "Israel is engaging in the deadliest and most deliberate effort to kill and silence journalists that CPJ has ever documented" and that there was a "pattern of journalists in Gaza reporting receiving threats, and subsequently, their family members being killed".[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387]

In a case filed with the [ICC](14880.md), the [RSF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reporters_sans_frontieres) accused the IDF of the targeted killing of seven journalists in Gaza between 22 October and 15 December 2023: Asem Al-Barsh, Bilal Jadallah, Montaser Al-Sawaf, Rushdi Al Siraj, Hassouna Salim, Sari Mansour, and Al Jazeera's Abudaqa. Targeting journalists is a war crime under Article 8 of the Rome Statute.[^cite_note-388]

The [PCHR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Centre_for_Human_Rights) alleges that this killing is to intimidate and deter journalists from coverage of current events.[^cite_note-Akila-383]

#### Medical workers

The systematic killing of Palestinian medical workers has been characterised as a central component of the genocide, sometimes termed "medicide" (the use of military force and state policy to systematically destroy a society's access to medical care).[^cite_note-389][^cite_note-390]

[Physicians for Human Rights Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physicians_for_Human_Rights_Israel) said the killing and detention of over 1,800 healthcare workers, including senior specialists, is not incidental to war but a deliberate strategy to decimate medical capacity and render future recovery nearly impossible and that this met the criteria for genocide.[^cite_note-391]

UN Special Rapporteur on the Right to Health [Tlaleng Mofokeng](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tlaleng_Mofokeng) called the assault on Gaza's medical sector "medicide" and a component of the ongoing genocide.[^cite_note-392][^cite_note-393]

## Board of Peace

Main article: [Board of Peace](81274522.md)

On 27 June 2026, *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* released a story about a draft resolution of the Board of Peace, which Omar Shakir of [DAWN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy_for_the_Arab_World_Now) said could potentially lead to complete immunity for Israel from any prosecution for crimes in Gaza and enable it to seize any land or property for its own use without recompense, adding: "Far from signaling an end to genocide, apartheid and occupation, this document suggests entrenching some of its ugliest signature characteristics. This risks not only complicity, but direct perpetration of grave abuses."[^cite_note-394]

On 30 June, the Board of Peace reportedly scheduled a pilot scheme to put Gaza civilians into camps overseen by a multilateral force.[^cite_note-Mansour-2026-07-395][^cite_note-396][^cite_note-397] The aim is to leave Hamas with "no people, no land, and no resources".[^cite_note-398][^cite_note-MEE-2026-07-399] Ahmed al-Tanani of the [Arab Center for Research and Policy Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Center_for_Research_and_Policy_Studies) said Hamas is demanding the mediators provide real guarantees to prevent humanitarian projects from turning into a "cover for genocide".[^cite_note-Mansour-2026-07-395]

## Academic and legal discourse

Main article: [Academic and legal responses to the Gaza genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academic_and_legal_responses_to_the_Gaza_genocide)

See also: [Palestinian genocide accusation § Academic debate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_genocide_accusation#Academic_debate), and [§ Legal debate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_genocide_accusation#Legal_debate)

Answers to the question "How would you define Israel's military campaign in Gaza?" among scholars specialized in Middle East studies in a January 31–February 19, 2025 survey.

[^cite_note-MESB_2025-400]

1. Genocide (46%)

2. Major war crimes akin to genocide (36%)

3. Major war crimes but not akin to genocide (9%)

4. Unjustified actions but not major war crimes (4%)

5. Justified actions under the right to self-\[defence] (4%)

6. I don't know (2%)

"Note: Percentages may not always add to one hundred due to rounding."

There is a growing consensus among genocide and Holocaust scholars, international legal experts, human rights organisations, and governments that Israel's actions in Gaza constitute genocide. Israel and its supporters deny the accusation.[^cite_note-401][^cite_note-Bouranova-402][^cite_note-MESB_2025-400]

### Relationship to Zionism and the Nakba

Palestinians have emphasized the relationship between the Gaza genocide and [Zionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionism) and its [political violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionist_political_violence), describing the Gaza genocide as a continuation of the [Nakba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nakba) and [ongoing Nakba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ongoing_Nakba).[^cite_note-403][^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405][^cite_note-406][^cite_note-407][^cite_note-408][^cite_note-409]

Some scholars argue that the Gaza genocide is merely the latest stage of a ["slow-motion genocide" of Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_genocide_allegations) that began with the Nakba and the [expulsion of hundreds of thousands of Palestinians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Palestinian_expulsion_and_flight) upon Israel's founding in 1948.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw2025b2-410][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELeVine20252-411][^cite_note-412]
