According to [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem) and others, the Gaza genocide is occurring in the context of over 75 years of [Zionist settler colonial](70418136.md) violence and [Jewish supremacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_supremacy) targeting Palestinians.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem20255-88][^cite_note-413] Multiple genocide scholars argue that [settler colonialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Settler_colonialism) is an important motive for Israel's actions.[^cite_note-414] [Raz Segal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raz_Segal) and B'Tselem also highlight the increasing influence of Jewish supremacy in Israeli politics,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESegal2025182-415][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem20255-88] while reports by [Francesca Albanese](72566699.md) and [Amos Goldberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amos_Goldberg) highlight the project of [Greater Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Israel).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlbanese2024a4-416][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlbanese2024b20–22-417][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoldberg20249-418]

### Genocide and Holocaust studies

In May 2025, *[NRC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NRC_\(newspaper\))* wrote that leading scholars in [genocide studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_studies) are "surprisingly unanimous" that Israel is committing genocide.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEvan_LaarhovenPeekWalters2025-419] Some scholars of [Holocaust studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_studies), such as [Norman J. W. Goda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman_J._W._Goda) and [Jeffrey Herf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeffrey_Herf), have said that Israel is not committing genocide.[^cite_note-420] Others, such as Israeli professors [Raz Segal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raz_Segal) and [Shira Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shira_Klein?action=edit\&redlink=1), have argued that Israel's actions should be analysed as a case of genocide,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEvan_LaarhovenPeekWalters2025-419][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlein20251–7-421][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESegal202462–66-422] citing, among other things, attacks on infrastructure, food, and water as genocidal.[^cite_note-423] [Omer Bartov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omer_Bartov), professor of Holocaust and genocide studies at [Brown University](4157.md) and an [Israel Defense Forces](39237.md) veteran, has said: "intent has been publicly expressed by numerous officials and leaders. But intent can also be derived from a pattern of operations on the ground, and this pattern became clear by May 2024".[^cite_note-Bartov_2025-424] [Amos Goldberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amos_Goldberg) and [Daniel Blatman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Blatman), historians of the Holocaust and genocide studies at the [Hebrew University of Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_University_of_Jerusalem), noted that "most acts of genocide are perceived by their perpetrators as acts of self-defense against their victims" and the Gaza war "falls into this category".[^cite_note-425]

On 31 August 2025, the [International Association of Genocide Scholars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Association_of_Genocide_Scholars) (IAGS), the world's largest academic association of genocide scholars, passed a resolution saying that Israel has committed genocide in Gaza.[^cite_note-IAGSReuters-26][^cite_note-IAGSResolution-27][^cite_note-426]

### Legal and human rights experts

|  | This section **contains an excessive amount of intricate [detail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:DETAIL)**. Specifically, from the overview detailing grouped positions, the section has quadrupled in size, seeking to include quotes from every person who has commented. Please help [improve it](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Gaza_genocide\&action=edit) by [spinning off](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Content_forks#Article_spinoffs:_.22Summary_style.22_meta-articles_and_summary_sections) or [relocating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Handling_trivia#Recommendations_for_handling_trivia) relevant information and removing excessive detail that goes against [Wikipedia's inclusion policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:What_Wikipedia_is_not). *(December 2025)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |
| --- | --- |

In December 2023, former International Criminal Court chief prosecutor [Luis Moreno Ocampo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luis_Moreno_Ocampo) told *[Al Jazeera](30204052.md)* that the siege of Gaza was a form of genocide due to Israel's imposing conditions that would lead to the deaths of Palestinians.[^cite_note-427] In December, in correspondence published in *The Lancet*, multiple specialists in international medicine and humanitarian aid reiterated warnings of the risk of genocide, while detailing how Israel's blocking of humanitarian support and aid were leading to unnecessary deaths, and how the death rate would continue to worsen. They called on signatories to the Genocide Convention to enforce a ceasefire on Israel.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFaddoulShannonAsgharBoukari202425–26-428] Nimer Sultany argues that, by mid-2024, a growing consensus among legal scholars suggested that Israel was committing genocide in Gaza.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESultany20244-429] Multiple public declarations from journals and academic organisations have warned of a potential genocide and declared opposition to an ongoing genocide.[^cite_note-twailr-155][^cite_note-430][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInternational_State_Crime_Initiative2023-431]

In January 2024, a number of prominent Israelis, represented by the human-rights lawyer [Michael Sfard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Sfard), sent Israel's attorney general and state prosecutor an open letter detailing examples of "the discourse of annihilation, expulsion and revenge".[^cite_note-Graham-Harrison_&_Kierszenbaum_2024-432] The signatories said the Israeli judiciary was ignoring incitement to genocide in Gaza.[^cite_note-Graham-Harrison_&_Kierszenbaum_2024-432] In January 2024, the American political scientist and international relations scholar [John Mearsheimer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Mearsheimer) wrote that, while he had believed during the first two months of the war that Israel was "guilty of serious war crimes", once the [2023 Gaza war ceasefire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Gaza_war_ceasefire) ended, it "became clear" to him "that Israeli leaders were in fact seeking to physically destroy a substantial portion of Gaza's Palestinian population".[^cite_note-433] [William Schabas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Schabas), an expert in [international criminal law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_criminal_law),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECurrie2011-434] said that South Africa's case was the strongest of all recent genocide cases at the [International Court of Justice](14918.md), citing the destruction of Gazan infrastructure and statements by Israeli politicians that Gazans are "human animals" and that Israel would deny them electricity, water, and medical care.[^cite_note-435][^cite_note-436][^cite_note-437]

On 26 January 2024, the ICJ issued a preliminary ruling finding that the rights asserted in [South Africa's filing](75736183.md) were "plausible", and an order requiring that Israel take all measures in its power to prevent acts of genocide, prevent and punish incitement to genocide, and allow basic humanitarian services into Gaza.[^cite_note-ICJruling-56][^cite_note-Donoghue-438] Judge ad hoc [Aharon Barak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aharon_Barak) dissented from the majority's view that genocide-related rights were "plausible", writing that South Africa had not demonstrated genocidal intent on Israel's part.[^cite_note-439]

On 15 May 2024, a report by the [University Network for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_Network_for_Human_Rights), [Boston University School of Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boston_University_School_of_Law), [Cornell Law School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cornell_Law_School), [University of Pretoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Pretoria), and the [Yale Law School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yale_Law_School) found that "Israel has committed genocidal acts".[^cite_note-Bouranova-402][^cite_note-440] In a May 2024 interview, Human Rights Watch co-founder [Aryeh Neier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aryeh_Neier) detailed how Israel's blocking of aid and the subsequent starvation of Gaza's population is indicative of genocide.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZakaria2024-441]

On 1 June 2024, professor of international law [Daniel-Erasmus Khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel-Erasmus_Khan?action=edit\&redlink=1) said there was no clear evidence of a special intent among Israeli leadership.[^cite_note-442] A June 2024 report by the University Network for Human Rights and Boston University School of Law found that "Israel has committed genocidal acts".[^cite_note-Bouranova-402] In an August 2024 op-ed, [Eli Rosenbaum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eli_Rosenbaum), a lawyer and former director of the [United States Department of Justice's Office of Special Investigations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_of_Special_Investigations_\(United_States_Department_of_Justice\)), wrote that Israel's actions in Gaza were not genocidal since it was aiming to "prevent genocide" by Hamas.[^cite_note-443]

In October 2024, legal scholar [Conor Gearty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conor_Gearty) called Israel genocidal, pointing to the continued attacks on schools and hospitals and the lack of internal investigations by Israeli authorities into potential crimes.[^cite_note-444] Grietje Baars, who advocates Marxist legal theory, and José Manuel Barreto, who advocates [Third World approaches to international law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_World_approaches_to_international_law), have argued that the international community's failure to treat Israel's actions in Gaza as genocide and respond accordingly has harmed the principles of the [international order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_order) and international law itself, and exposed the deficiencies of international governance.[^cite_note-Baars_2024-445][^cite_note-Barreto_2024-446] Legal scholar José Manuel Barreto argues that "the Palestinian genocide has unveiled the deep colonial structure of the international legal order" and identifies events in Gaza with the history of genocides in the colonised world, which he says the [Westphalian system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westphalian_system) has historically failed to prevent.[^cite_note-Barreto_2024-446]

In December 2024, Amnesty International published a report accusing Israel of committing genocide.[^cite_note-447][^cite_note-448][^cite_note-449] After the Amnesty report, Human Rights Watch also said Israel had committed "genocidal acts" and that Israeli officials statements may indicate genocidal intent.[^cite_note-450]

In April 2025, the barrister [Michael Mansfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Mansfield) said there was "no question" that genocide was occurring.[^cite_note-451] In May 2025, Luigi Daniele, a lecturer at [Nottingham Law School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nottingham_Law_School), noted a link between the IDF's justification for its conduct in Gaza and the [Rapid Support Forces](43053874.md) rationale in the [Sudanese civil war](73556699.md), saying it "reveals the emergence of a template to commit mass extermination and even genocide".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENashed2025-452] In May 2024 the legal scholar Nimer Sultany supported [Forensic Architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forensic_Architecture)'s assessment that Israel had weaponised international humanitarian law into "humanitarian violence".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESultany202410–11-453] This was supported in July by legal scholar [Neve Gordon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neve_Gordon) and the anthropologist [Nicola Perugini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicola_Perugini), who argued that Israel used "the law itself as a tool legitimizing genocide".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPeruginiGordon2024-454]

In June 2025, Ambos and scholar [Stefanie Bock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefanie_Bock?action=edit\&redlink=1) wrote that it has become more difficult to deny genocidal intent.[^cite_note-455]

In July 2025, 1,300 professionals and academics in public health, health care, and the social sciences signed a letter acknowledging the Gaza genocide.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDe_VogliMontomoliAbu-SittahPappé20251-456] According to peace studies scholar Ernesto Verdeja, even by "the most inflexible interpretation of genocide, Gaza qualifies as genocidal".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVerdeja20258-457] In July 2025, *[Le Monde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Monde)* reported that legal experts remained divided on the question, with some agreeing that Israel was committing genocide and others viewing the charge as unsubstantiated.[^cite_note-Ayad_2025-68] Bartov wrote that the silence of many scholars of the Holocaust and Holocaust commemoration institutions and their disbelief that Israel could commit war crimes, crimes against humanity, ethnic cleansing, or genocide threatens [universalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universalist) interpretations of Holocaust studies and Holocaust commemoration and may lead to a decline in the relevance of Holocaust education.[^cite_note-458][^cite_note-Bartov_2025-424] Journalist Colin Jones interviewed lawyers affiliated with the US military and concluded that they see Gaza as a test case for what military conduct might be acceptable in a hypothetical war between the US and a peer power such as China.[^cite_note-Jones_2025-459]

In August 2025, according to Mia Swart, a growing consensus was emerging "in international legal circles that Israel is committing genocide".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESwart20253-460] [Craig Mokhiber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Craig_Mokhiber), a retired UN human rights lawyer, wrote, "Never, in the modern era, have we seen such a clear-cut, article-by-article violation of the United Nations Genocide Convention, so broad a consensus in the identification of the crime".[^cite_note-Mokhiber-461]

### Other scholars

In March 2024, the [Middle East Studies Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_Studies_Association) condemned the "accelerating scale of genocidal violence being inflicted on the Palestinian population of Gaza", saying that Israel's conduct constituted [cultural genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_genocide).[^cite_note-462] Surveys of 758 Middle East scholars by the [Brookings Institution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookings_Institution) indicated a growing consensus that Israel's military campaign in Gaza was genocide.[^cite_note-463][^cite_note-464][^cite_note-MESB_2025-400]

In 2025, [John Spencer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Spencer_\(military_officer\)), chair of urban warfare studies at the [Modern War Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_War_Institute) at the [United States Military Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Military_Academy), argued that Israel had not demonstrated genocidal intent, based on what he called "evidence of what Israel is doing to preserve infrastructure, civilian life, to provide services".[^cite_note-465]

[Robert Satloff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Satloff) of [The Washington Institute for Near East Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Washington_Institute_for_Near_East_Policy) criticized the IAGS genocide resolution for its sourcing and for lacking independent research, noting that it passed with the support of "only one-fifth ... of the organization's membership", though he wrote that his "critique of the IAGS resolution should not be misread as endorsement of Israeli strategy and tactics in Gaza or indifference to the terrible human toll in this conflict".[^cite_note-466]

## Legal proceedings

### International Court of Justice application

Main article: [South Africa's genocide case against Israel](75736183.md)



Stances of states on [*South Africa v. Israel*](75736183.md):

&#x20; South Africa

&#x20; Countries that have supported South Africa's case

&#x20; Countries that have opposed South Africa's case

&#x20; Israel

In December 2023, [South Africa](17416221.md) instituted proceedings at the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) pursuant to the [Genocide Convention](143988.md), accusing Israel of committing genocide, war crimes, and crimes against humanity against Palestinians in Gaza.[^cite_note-Chao-Fong_et_al._2023-467][^cite_note-ICJ-2023-12-29-55][^cite_note-AP_2023-468] South African president [Cyril Ramaphosa](1089194.md) [compared Israel's actions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_apartheid) to [apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apartheid).[^cite_note-469] Several [international organisations and other nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa_v._Israel_\(Genocide_Convention\)#Other_international_responses) supported South Africa's suit.[^cite_note-470][^cite_note-Progressive_International_2024-471]

In its application, South Africa argued that Israel's actions "are genocidal in character because they are intended to bring about the destruction of a substantial part of the Palestinian national, racial and ethnical group".[^cite_note-ICJ-2023-12-29-55][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECorder2024-472] South Africa requested that the ICJ issue an [interim order](17489152.md) requiring Israel to "immediately suspend its military operations in and against Gaza".[^cite_note-ICJ-2023-12-29-55][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECorder2024-472]

On 26 January 2024, the ICJ issued a preliminary ruling finding that the rights asserted in South Africa's filing were "plausible" and issued an order requiring that Israel take all measures in its power to prevent acts of genocide, prevent and punish incitement to genocide, and allow basic humanitarian services into Gaza.[^cite_note-ICJruling-56][^cite_note-Donoghue-438] Later that year, South Africa asked the ICJ to order additional measures against Israel,[^cite_note-473] and in May, the court issued what some experts considered to be an ambiguous order but which was widely understood as requiring Israel to immediately halt its [offensive in Rafah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rafah_offensive).[^cite_note-474] Israel rejected this interpretation and continued its offensive.[^cite_note-Casciani_2024b-59]

The Israeli government spokesman [Eylon Levy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eylon_Levy) rejected the allegations "with disgust"[^cite_note-AP_2023-468] and accused South Africa of cooperating with Hamas,[^cite_note-Chao-Fong_et_al._2023-467] calling South Africa's claims "[blood libel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_libel)".[^cite_note-475] On 2 January 2024, Israel decided to appear before the ICJ in response to South Africa's case, despite a history of ignoring international tribunals.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECorder2024-472] On 13 January, Netanyahu said, "No one will stop us. Not The Hague, not the [Axis of Evil](36658251.md), no one."[^cite_note-476] Israeli officials accused the court of antisemitic bias.[^cite_note-McKernan_2024-477][^cite_note-478] Israel asserted that it had taken all possible measures to safeguard civilians during its military campaign in Gaza.[^cite_note-479][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcDoom20245–6-480] Some [left-wing Israeli politicians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left-wing_politics_in_Israel), including [Ofer Cassif](60517491.md), supported South Africa's case.[^cite_note-481]

International Court of Justice Vice President [Julia Sebutinde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_Sebutinde) was one of the 17 judges ruling on provisional measures in [South Africa's genocide case against Israel](75736183.md). She voted against all the provisional measures, and was the only [permanent judge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judges_of_the_International_Court_of_Justice#Permanent_judges) to vote against any of the measures.[^cite_note-482][^cite_note-jta-20240126-483] In her dissenting opinion, Sebutinde wrote, "multiple concrete actions were taken by Israel to facilitate the provision of humanitarian aid for the civilian population of Gaza."[^cite_note-484] She was [accused of plagiarizing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julia_Sebutinde#Plagiarism_controversy) large parts of her dissenting opinion from pro-Israel sources,[^cite_note-485][^cite_note-486] [Wikipedia](5043734.md), and the [BBC](19344654.md). According to political scientist [Norman Finkelstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norman_Finkelstein), "at least 32 percent of Sebutinde's dissent was plagiarised", including from writings by [Douglas Feith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_Feith).[^cite_note-487]

### International Criminal Court

Main articles: [International Criminal Court investigation in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court_investigation_in_Palestine) and [International Criminal Court arrest warrants for Israeli leaders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court_arrest_warrants_for_Israeli_leaders)

In May 2024, Khan applied for arrest warrants against Israeli Prime Minister Netanyahu and Minister of Defence Yoav Gallant, saying he had reasonable grounds to believe they bore criminal responsibility for war crimes and crimes against humanity committed in Gaza.[^cite_note-488] The list of crimes did not include genocide, which is legally distinct from [extermination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extermination_\(crime\)).[^cite_note-489] The warrants were issued in November 2024.[^cite_note-El_Abdallah_2024-290]

As part of a December 2024 report accusing Israel of genocide, Amnesty International called on the ICC "to urgently consider the commission of the crime of genocide by Israeli officials since 7 October 2023 in the ongoing investigation into the situation in the State of Palestine".[^cite_note-AmnestyGenocideConclusion-490][^cite_note-491] Also in December 2024, the Israeli law professor [Omer Shatz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Omer_Shatz) filed a complaint with the ICC naming eight Israeli political and media figures he believed were responsible for incitement to genocide.[^cite_note-492] On 1 October 2025, a complaint was filed with the ICC accusing Italian Prime Minister [Giorgia Meloni](29228976.md) and other officials of genocide for their support of Israel.[^cite_note-493][^cite_note-494] The US and Israel have politically pressured and imposed sanctions on the ICC due to its positions during the war.[^cite_note-495]

### Other proceedings

Main articles: [Defense for Children International – Palestine v. Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_for_Children_International_%E2%80%93_Palestine_v._Biden) and [Nicaragua v. Germany](76228006.md)

In November 2023, the [Center for Constitutional Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Constitutional_Rights) sued US President [Joe Biden](145422.md), Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken), and Secretary of Defence [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin),[^cite_note-Mansoor_2023-496][^cite_note-497][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESegalDaniele20246-99] alleging that Israel's "mass killings", targeting of civilian infrastructure, and forced expulsions amount to genocide,[^cite_note-McGreal_2023-161][^cite_note-Mansoor_2023-496] and that the US was capable of deterring Israel from committing these acts due to the countries' close relationship.[^cite_note-McGreal_2023-161] A [federal judge](834368.md) dismissed the case in January 2024, ruling that the [US Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Constitution) prevented his court from determining foreign policy, but writing that "as the ICJ has found, it is plausible that Israel's conduct amounts to genocide".[^cite_note-Hubler_2024-498] The judge also commented that he would have preferred to have issued the injunction and urged Biden to rethink US policy.[^cite_note-499][^cite_note-Hanson_2023-500][^cite_note-Mansoor_2023-496]

In February 2024, lawyers representing Palestinians in Germany filed a criminal complaint against politicians including Chancellor [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz), Foreign Minister [Annalena Baerbock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annalena_Baerbock), Economic Minister [Robert Habeck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Habeck), and Finance Minister [Christian Lindner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Lindner) for "aiding and abetting" genocide in Gaza.[^cite_note-Schaer_2024-501][^cite_note-mem-2024-02-23-502] The [Public Prosecutor General](12141070.md) dismissed the complaint due to lack of [reasonable suspicion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reasonable_suspicion).[^cite_note-lto-2025-09-20-503] The lawyers filed a similar complaint against current and former German government members in September 2025.[^cite_note-lto-2025-09-20-503][^cite_note-504]

In March 2024, the Nicaraguan government initiated proceedings against Germany at the ICJ under the Genocide Convention concerning Germany's support for Israel in the Gaza war.[^cite_note-mee-2024-1-1-505][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEvan_den_Berg2024-506] It sought [provisional measures of protection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provisional_measures_of_protection), including resumption of suspended German funding of the [UNRWA](32270.md) and cessation of military supplies to Israel.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEvan_den_Berg2024-506]

In March 2024, Birchgrove Legal referred Australian Prime Minister [Anthony Albanese](556745.md), Foreign Minister [Penny Wong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penny_Wong), Opposition Leader [Peter Dutton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Dutton), and others to the ICC as accessories to genocide, war crimes, and crimes against humanity, citing the defunding of UNRWA, the provision of military aid, and "unequivocal political support" for Israel's actions during the Gaza war.[^cite_note-Wedesweiler_2024-507][^cite_note-Rothwell_2024-508]

In November 2025, the Chief Public Prosecutor's Office in [Turkey](11125639.md) issued arrest warrants for 37 Israeli suspects on charges of genocide and crimes against humanity. Officials named in the Turkish indictment included Netanyahu, security minister [Itamar Ben-Gvir](54504115.md), defence minister [Israel Katz](19041378.md) (who had replaced Gallant), and IDF chief of staff [Eyal Zamir](61877756.md).[^cite_note-509]

## Responsibility of third states and other entities

See also: [Complicity in genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complicity_in_genocide) and [Responsibility to protect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Responsibility_to_protect)

All UN states that are signatories to the Genocide Convention are obliged "to employ all means reasonably available to them, so as to prevent genocide so far as possible" and must not provide "means to enable or facilitate the commission of the crime".[^cite_note-510] Multiple scholars argue that the inaction of the international community to confirmed atrocities in Gaza expose the irrelevance and weakness of the [Responsibility to Protect (R2P) doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Responsibility_to_protect).[^cite_note-511][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESirleaf2024182-512][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVerdeja202516–18-513] Many Western countries — especially the United States, Germany, and the United Kingdom — support Israel by offering diplomatic, military, and intelligence support.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw2025a416–422-514] Some journalists,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEScahillGrim2024-515][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELevitz2023-516] scholars,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBeaklini2023-517][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAyyash2023-518][^cite_note-Lakhani_&_Niranjan_2024-519] commentators,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPorras2023-520] and UN officials[^cite_note-521][^cite_note-522] say the actions of Western countries make them [complicit in genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complicity_in_genocide).

The [Independent International Commission of Inquiry on Palestine](69971286.md) said, "States may be complicit in failing to prevent genocide if they do not act in compliance" with the [International Court of Justice's](75736183.md) orders or if they directly aid or assist in "the commission of genocide".[^cite_note-523] A [UN Special Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Committee_to_Investigate_Israeli_Practices_Affecting_the_Human_Rights_of_the_Palestinian_People) wrote, "Failing to act now ... will tear apart the very foundation of the international rule of law we have collectively built to protect peace, security, and the well-being of all. Our inaction today is setting a perilous precedent for tomorrow."[^cite_note-UNCommitteeHeadGenocide-25]

### United States

See also: [United States support for Israel in the Gaza war](65284610.md) and [Defense for Children International – Palestine v. Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_for_Children_International_%E2%80%93_Palestine_v._Biden)



[United States Air Force](32090.md) airmen prepare for departure in a [C-17 Globemaster III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-17_Globemaster_III) to [supply Israel](65284610.md) with munitions, Oct. 15, 2023, at [Ramstein Air Base](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramstein_Air_Base), Germany.

Multiple sources—including scholars,[^cite_note-524][^cite_note-525] journalists,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELevitz2023-516] think tanks,[^cite_note-526] the [Lemkin Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lemkin_Institute),[^cite_note-527] [Human Rights Watch](46530.md),[^cite_note-528] and others[^cite_note-529]—say that the United States is complicit in the genocide.&#x20;

The United States has been a key provider of diplomatic and material support before and during the Gaza war.[^cite_note-530][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWezemanDjokicGeorgeHussain202411–12-531] It pays for around 15% of Israel's military budget and is its largest supplier of arms; it provided more than \$22.8 billion to the war effort in its first year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBachmanRuiz20255-532]

Both the [Biden](65732174.md) and [Trump administrations](78298108.md) have denied the genocide in Gaza,[^cite_note-newlines-533] but by September 2025 six members of the [US Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Congress) had recognised the genocide: [Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandria_Ocasio-Cortez), [Rashida Tlaib](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rashida_Tlaib), [Summer Lee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Summer_Lee), [Marjorie Taylor Greene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marjorie_Taylor_Greene), [Becca Balint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Becca_Balint), and [Bernie Sanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernie_Sanders).[^cite_note-Sanders092025-534] [John Kirby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Kirby_\(admiral\)), the [U.S. National Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._National_Security_Council) spokesman, described by Israeli media outlet *[Ynet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ynet)* as "an exceptionally accomplished Israeli advocate",[^cite_note-535] said: "Israel's trying to defend itself against a genocidal terrorist threat. So if we're going to start using that word, fine, let's use it appropriately".[^cite_note-Forbes-536]

In November 2023, critics of President [Joe Biden](145422.md)'s support of Israel coined the nickname "Genocide Joe", which became popular among pro-Palestinian protesters.[^cite_note-537][^cite_note-Forbes-536] On 13 November 2023, the New York-based [Center for Constitutional Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_for_Constitutional_Rights) sued Biden for allegedly failing in his legal duties to prevent Israel from committing genocide in Gaza in the Gaza war.[^cite_note-McGreal_2023-161][^cite_note-Mansoor_2023-496] In the lawsuit, genocide scholar [William Schabas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Schabas) wrote that the US was "in breach of its obligation" under the 1948 [Genocide Convention](143988.md) and international law.[^cite_note-Mansoor_2023-496][^cite_note-Hanson_2023-500] In January 2024, a [federal judge](834368.md) dismissed the case, [Defense for Children International – Palestine v. Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defense_for_Children_International_%E2%80%93_Palestine_v._Biden), saying the [Constitution](31644.md) prevented his court from determining foreign policy, which is reserved to the political branches of the U.S. government, though he would have preferred to have issued the injunction and urged Biden to rethink U.S. policy, writing, "it is plausible that Israel's conduct amounts to genocide."[^cite_note-Hubler_2024-498] On 4 January 2024, the U.S. government acknowledged it was not formally assessing whether Israel was violating international humanitarian law.[^cite_note-538] In September 2024, it was reported that the Biden administration ignored or suppressed several memos from its own governmental departments describing human rights abuses in Gaza.[^cite_note-539][^cite_note-540]

In February 2025, President [Donald Trump proposed a U.S. takeover of Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Donald_Trump_Gaza_Strip_takeover_proposal) and the displacement of its population.[^cite_note-newlines-533]

### United Kingdom

See also: [United Kingdom and the Gaza war](76740255.md) and [Israel–United Kingdom relations](20968669.md)



Protester holding a sign claiming the [UK Labour Party](19279158.md) leader [Keir Starmer](18681491.md) (image blurred due to copyright) supports genocide

The British government issues [export licences](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UK_arms_export) for British companies to sell weapons to Israel.[^cite_note-Holden_&_James_2024-541] Multiple sources—including [Human Rights Watch](46530.md),[^cite_note-542] [Save the Children UK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Save_the_Children_UK),[^cite_note-543] lawyers, and scholars[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELawless2024-544][^cite_note-545]—say that the UK is complicit in the genocide because the UK government continues to issue companies licences to export weapons to Israel despite Israel's conduct in Gaza.[^cite_note-Holden_&_James_2024-541][^cite_note-546]

In December 2023, Scottish First Minister [Humza Yousaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humza_Yousaf) condemned the UK's abstention from a draft UN resolution calling for a ceasefire in Gaza, saying this would lead to the deaths of more children.[^cite_note-547]

On 2 September 2024, Foreign Secretary [David Lammy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Lammy) announced he was suspending approximately 30 arms export licences to Israel after a government review concluded there was a high risk these exports were being used for severe violations of international humanitarian law.[^cite_note-548] At the time of the suspension, Israel had around 350 arms export licences in the UK.[^cite_note-549]

On 3 September 2025, the [Scottish Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scottish_Government) announced a ban on funding to "arms companies whose products or services are provided to countries where there is plausible evidence of genocide being committed", including Israel.[^cite_note-550] The [Scottish Parliament](61188.md) also voted for a boycott of Israel and any companies providing it military support.[^cite_note-551]

In February 2026, noting that over 2,000 UK nationals had served in the IDF during the genocide, the Public Interest Law Centre said the UK had a responsibility to investigate and prosecute where there is sufficient evidence.[^cite_note-552]

In December 2023, human rights groups [Al-Haq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Haq) and Global Legal Action Network sued the British government, arguing that granting export licences for military equipment to Israel risked breaching the Genocide Convention. In June 2025, the [High Court of Justice](26569402.md) dismissed the case, ruling that the UK's continued participation in the [F-35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-35) programme—despite some components reaching Israel through a global spares pool—was lawful and partly beyond the scope of domestic courts.[^cite_note-553][^cite_note-554][^cite_note-555][^cite_note-556]

### European Union

See also: [Israel–European Union relations § Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93European_Union_relations#Gaza_war)

The European Union was accused of potential complicity after it did not suspend the [EU-Israel Association Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union_Association_Agreement). Secretary General of Amnesty International [Agnès Callamard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agn%C3%A8s_Callamard) said the EU was giving Israel a "green light" to continue its genocide and was at "risk of complicity in Israel's actions".[^cite_note-557][^cite_note-558] [The Rights Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Rights_Forum?action=edit\&redlink=1) said the decision was "shameless complicity in genocide".[^cite_note-559] In March 2024, [Oxfam](19698439.md) and several other non-governmental organisations released a statement detailing their intention[^cite_note-560] to sue Denmark to prevent arms sales to Israel, warning that by selling arms Denmark was "complicit in violations of international humanitarian law ... and a plausible genocide".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOxfam2024-561][^cite_note-562]

#### Germany

See also: [German support for Israel in the Gaza war](78816942.md)

In October 2023, political analyst Lena Obermaier argued that Germany is complicit in Israel's war crimes against Gaza.[^cite_note-Obermaier_2023-563] She detailed how most of Germany's most prominent news outlets have "been silent on Israeli genocidal policies". She also highlighted police suppression of pro-Palestine protests[^cite_note-564] as evidence of state complicity.[^cite_note-Obermaier_2023-563] Accusing Israel of genocide is not illegal in Germany, but according to the European Legal Support Center, a nonprofit aligned with the Palestinian solidarity movement,[^cite_note-565] at least one protester has been arrested after doing so.[^cite_note-566] [Germany's Federal Commissioner for the Fight against Antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-antisemitism_in_Germany#Creation_of_antisemitism_commissioners) [Felix Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felix_Klein_\(German_antisemitism_commissioner\)) has called accusations of genocide against Israel antisemitic since they lead to [demonisation and delegitimation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Ds_of_antisemitism) of the country.[^cite_note-567][^cite_note-568]

In February 2024 lawyers representing Palestinians in Germany filed a criminal complaint with the [Public Prosecutor General](12141070.md) against various senior politicians including [Olaf Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz), [Annalena Baerbock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annalena_Baerbock), [Robert Habeck](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Habeck), and [Christian Lindner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Lindner) for "aiding and abetting" the genocide in Gaza.[^cite_note-Schaer_2024-501][^cite_note-mem-2024-02-23-502] On 1 March 2024, [Nicaragua instituted proceedings against Germany](76228006.md) at the ICJ under the Genocide Convention concerning Germany's support for Israel in the Gaza war.[^cite_note-mee-2024-1-1-505][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEvan_den_Berg2024-506]

#### Italy

See also: [Israel–Italy relations](20570993.md)

As of May 2025, Italy was Israel's third-largest supplier of weaponry, representing around 1% of Israel's weaponry imports.[^cite_note-569][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWezemanDjokicGeorgeHussain2024-570] Despite the Italian government's claims that weaponry supplement to Israel had been interrupted, [Leonardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_\(company\)), one of Italy's biggest arms companies, confirmed that the Italian government and Italian companies still exported arms to Israel.[^cite_note-571] Francesca Albanese directly called Italy an accomplice in the Gaza genocide.[^cite_note-572]

On 7 October 2025, Prime Minister [Giorgia Meloni](29228976.md) said in an interview with *[RAI – Radiotelevisione italiana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAI)* that a pro-Palestinian advocacy group had reported her, Defence Minister [Guido Crosetto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guido_Crosetto), Foreign Minister [Antonio Tajani](1767298.md), and [Leonardo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_\(company\)) director [Roberto Cingolani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Cingolani) to the ICC for alleged complicity in the genocide.[^cite_note-573][^cite_note-574] The complaint, which was signed by 50 people, including law professors, lawyers, and several public figures, was filed on 1 October. It accused Meloni's government of complicity for supplying arms to Israel and urged the ICC to evaluate whether a formal investigation into the allegation could be initiated.[^cite_note-575]

### Egypt

See also: [Egypt and the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt_and_the_Gaza_war) and [Egypt–Israel relations](21337265.md)

Egypt has been accused of complicity in genocide for its enforcement of the [blockade on Gaza](75013719.md) by refusing to open the [Rafah Border Crossing](10475618.md).[^cite_note-Mahmoud_2024-576][^cite_note-577] Egypt was also accused of complicity with Israel for its crackdown on the [Global March to Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_March_to_Gaza).[^cite_note-578]

In April 2025, [Cage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cage_\(organisation\)) and five other African advocacy organisations filed a complaint with the [African Commission on Human and Peoples' Rights](1620260.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECage2025-579] Egypt is not permitted to turn people away at its borders if doing so would expose them to life-threatening conditions or collective punishment, as this would violate its obligations under the [International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Covenant_on_Civil_and_Political_Rights) and the [Convention Against Torture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_Against_Torture).[^cite_note-580][^cite_note-581]

Egypt has said that it closed the Rafah crossing in opposition to Israeli plans to displace Palestinians.[^cite_note-Mahmoud_2024-576] It rejected an Israeli proposal that Gaza be [returned to Egypt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Occupation_of_the_Gaza_Strip_by_the_United_Arab_Republic) for up to 15 years[^cite_note-582] and formally supported South Africa's genocide case against Israel.[^cite_note-583]

### India

See also: [India–Israel relations](7199609.md)

On 30 July 2026, Amnesty International published "Made in India: The Supply of Weapons and Ammunition to Israel", a report that revealed India's military support to Israel despite the genocide.[^cite_note-584][^cite_note-585] According to the report, since 7 October 2023, companies directly owned and controlled by the Indian government have sent Israel at least 2,596 weapons shipments containing ammunition, parts and accessories.[^cite_note-india-586] Amnesty International also reported that at least 390,516 small arms parts for military-grade weapons, 564,970 explosive ordnance parts, and 298 military vehicle components were sent to defence companies in Israel.[^cite_note-587] Amnesty International Secretary General [Agnès Callamard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agn%C3%A8s_Callamard) said that India's transfer of arms to Israel violates its duty to prevent genocide under the Genocide Convention and demanded that India respect the Geneva Conventions.[^cite_note-india-586]

### Other states

In March 2024, the Sydney-based firm Birchgrove Legal Australian referred Prime Minister [Anthony Albanese](556745.md), Foreign Minister [Penny Wong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penny_Wong), Opposition Leader [Peter Dutton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Dutton), and others to the ICC as accessories to genocide, war crimes, and crimes against humanity, citing the defunding of [UNRWA](32270.md), the provision of military aid, and "unequivocal political support" for Israel's actions during the Gaza war.[^cite_note-Wedesweiler_2024-507][^cite_note-Rothwell_2024-508]

According to the [CCPA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Centre_for_Policy_Alternatives), Canada is complicit in the genocide because it has allowed military exports to Israel to continue under previously approved permits despite publicly saying that new arms sales were halted, and has not implemented a comprehensive arms embargo, leaving loopholes such as third-country transfers in place.[^cite_note-588]

### Private sector and media

See also: [List of companies involved in the Gaza war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_companies_involved_in_the_Gaza_war)

Multiple corporations have been accused of profiting from the Gaza genocide.[^cite_note-589][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlbanese20251–2-590] [Lockheed Martin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Martin), [General Dynamics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Dynamics), and [RTX Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTX_Corporation) executives have called the war in Gaza a source of increased profits.[^cite_note-591][^cite_note-592] From October 2023 to July 2025, RTX's stock price rose by 77%.[^cite_note-593]

On 20 June 2024, UN experts warned that continued arms transfers to Israel could violate international law and risk state and corporate complicity in genocide. They called for an immediate halt to all weapons transfers to Israel, including by such major arms manufacturers as [BAE Systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BAE_Systems), [Boeing](18933266.md), Lockheed Martin, and [Caterpillar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caterpillar_Inc.). They also warned institutions investing in these companies, such as [Bank of America](347756.md), [BlackRock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BlackRock), [Citigroup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Citigroup), and [Wells Fargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wells_Fargo).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOHCHR2024c-594]

In June 2025, a UN expert's report named [Alphabet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alphabet_\(company\)), [Amazon](90451.md), [Microsoft](19001.md), and [IBM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBM) as "central to Israel's surveillance apparatus and the ongoing Gaza destruction", and [Palantir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palantir) as a source of AI tools for the Israeli military.[^cite_note-UN-corporate-report-595] The report also said [Allianz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allianz), [Barclays](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barclays), BlackRock, and [BNP Paribas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BNP_Paribas) had [underwritten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Underwriting) and purchased Israeli government bonds, which the UN said are the main source of financing for Israel's military expenditures.[^cite_note-596]

Scholars, journalists, media analysts, and human rights advocates have accused various media outlets of complicity through [media imperialism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_imperialism).[^cite_note-597] Brazilian journalist Bruno Lima Rocha Beaklini has accused Brazilian media outlets of supporting genocide against Palestinians.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBeaklini2023-517] Media analyst Adam H. Johnson has written that the Gaza genocide would have been impossible to sustain without the ongoing complicity of the U.S. corporate media.[^cite_note-598]

## Political recognition

### World leaders and governments

Main article: [Gaza genocide recognition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaza_genocide_recognition)



Positions of national governments on whether Israel is committing genocide in the Gaza war

&#x20; Agree

&#x20; Disagree

&#x20; Unclear

&#x20; Defer to the ruling in the [ICJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ICJ) case [*South Africa v. Israel*](75736183.md)

&#x20; No data

Several Western governments (notably the United States, United Kingdom, and Germany) reject calling Israel's actions in Gaza a genocide.[^cite_note-599][^cite_note-600][^cite_note-601] Belgium, Norway, and Canada have said they will abide by or await the ICJ's judgment rather than take a definitive stance.[^cite_note-602][^cite_note-603][^cite_note-604]

A large group of states, especially in the [Islamic world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_world), most of Africa, Latin America, and some European countries, explicitly describe Israel's actions as genocide and/or have joined or supported South Africa's ICJ case.[^cite_note-605] These include Ghana,[^cite_note-606] Turkey,[^cite_note-607] Malaysia,[^cite_note-608] Egypt,[^cite_note-609] Brazil,[^cite_note-610] Chile,[^cite_note-611] Spain,[^cite_note-612] Ireland,[^cite_note-613] and Slovenia.[^cite_note-614] On November 7, 2025, Turkey issued arrest warrants for genocide against Netanyahu and 36 colleagues.[^cite_note-615] Colombia initially supported the case, but withdrew its support to the case to restore the [Colombia–Israel relations](15647808.md).[^cite_note-616]

The [Organisation of Islamic Cooperation](47725.md) (OIC), the [Arab League](52625.md), and the [African Union](10634933.md) have collectively endorsed the characterisation of Israel's actions as genocide and called for accountability and measures such as ending arms transfers.[^cite_note-617] In January 2025, the [Hague Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hague_Group) convened in [Bogotá](211271.md) with the aim to uphold international rulings on Gaza, end arms shipments to Israel and build a pathway to broader recognition of [Palestinian statehood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_statehood).[^cite_note-618]

### Non-governmental organisations and intergovernmental organisations

Main article: [List of humanitarian and human rights groups accusing Israel of genocide in Gaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_humanitarian_and_human_rights_groups_accusing_Israel_of_genocide_in_Gaza)

Numerous non-governmental organisations and intergovernmental organisations have accused Israel of genocide. Among the first to warn of a risk of genocide, in mid-October 2023, were [Genocide Watch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocide_Watch) and the [Lemkin Institute for Genocide Prevention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lemkin_Institute_for_Genocide_Prevention).[^cite_note-619][^cite_note-620] Several Palestinian human rights groups, including [Defence for Children International](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_for_Children_International) (DCI),[^cite_note-621] [Al-Haq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Haq), [Al Mezan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Mezan), and the [Palestinian Centre for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Centre_for_Human_Rights), have filed lawsuits alleging a genocide.[^cite_note-sccoip-622] Some Israeli human rights organisations, such as [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem20254,_86-623] and [Physicians for Human Rights–Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physicians_for_Human_Rights%E2%80%93Israel), released reports calling Israel's campaign in Gaza a genocide.[^cite_note-Kottasová_&_Salman-31][^cite_note-624][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem202587-625] International human rights groups condemning Israel's genocide include the [International Federation for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Federation_for_Human_Rights),[^cite_note-FIDH_2023-32] [Euro-Mediterranean Human Rights Monitor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euro-Mediterranean_Human_Rights_Monitor),[^cite_note-MEE-2023-12-26-626] [Amnesty International](18947898.md),[^cite_note-AmnestyGenocideConclusion-490][^cite_note-627][^cite_note-Amnesty_International_Dec_2024-28] the [European Center for Constitutional and Human Rights](31683151.md),[^cite_note-628] and [Human Rights Watch](46530.md).[^cite_note-Berg2024-335] In October 2024, [Oxfam](19698439.md) and 37 other humanitarian organisations warned that Israel was failing to comply with the Genocide Convention as it wiped Northern Gaza "off the map".[^cite_note-629] *[Médecins Sans Frontières](20498.md)* (MSF) has also found that Israel is committing genocide.[^cite_note-630][^cite_note-631][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMédecins_Sans_Frontières2025-632] The American activist group [Jewish Voice for Peace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Voice_for_Peace) has said that Israel is committing genocide.[^cite_note-633][^cite_note-634][^cite_note-635]

In September 2025, the [UN Human Rights Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UN_Human_Rights_Council)'s [Independent International Commission of Inquiry](69971286.md) issued a report concluding that Israel is committing genocide against Palestinians in Gaza. The Commission found reasonable grounds to determine that Israeli authorities and security forces have committed, and continue to commit, four of the five genocidal acts defined under the 1948 Genocide Convention: killing members of the group; causing serious bodily or mental harm; deliberately inflicting conditions of life calculated to bring about the group's physical destruction in whole or in part; and imposing measures intended to prevent births.[^cite_note-UNCommissionGenocide-24][^cite_note-636]

## Cultural discourse

Main article: [Cultural discourse about the Gaza genocide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_discourse_about_the_Gaza_genocide)

Israel's genocide against the Palestinian people in Gaza has been a contentious topic in cultural discourse. Celebrities, athletes, public intellectuals, activists, cultural institutions and ordinary people have weighed in on the events in Gaza, as well as on the cultural and societal implications of viewing those events through the framework of genocide.[^cite_note-637]

### Public opinion on Israel's actions in Gaza

Multiple surveys have found broad support among Jewish Israelis for the government's policies toward Gaza.[^cite_note-HaaretzTransferPoll-638][^cite_note-HaaretzFaminePoll-639][^cite_note-640] An August 2025 Accord Center poll found that 76% of Jewish Israelis agreed with the statement "there are no innocent people in Gaza".[^cite_note-641] A May 2025 survey found that 82% supported expelling Gazans, while 56% favoured also expelling Palestinians from Israel.[^cite_note-HaaretzTransferPoll-638] Another August 2025 poll found that 79% of Jewish Israelis were untroubled by reports of famine in Gaza.[^cite_note-HaaretzFaminePoll-639] According to the Lemkin Institute for Genocide Prevention, "the vast majority of Israelis agree with...\[Israel's] plan and clearly will support anything to see it realized – from [apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_apartheid) to extermination."[^cite_note-642]

An August 2025 poll found that half of U.S. voters believed Israel was committing genocide in Gaza and 60% opposed further military assistance.[^cite_note-643] A September 2025 [AP](18935732.md)-[NORC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NORC_at_the_University_of_Chicago) poll found that the number of Americans who believe that Israel's military actions in Gaza have "gone too far" was increasing.[^cite_note-644]

An October 2025 *[Washington Post](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_Post)* poll found that most American Jews believe Israel is committing war crimes and 39% believe it is committing genocide.[^cite_note-645] A [Quinnipiac poll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quinnipiac_poll) found that 50% of U.S. voters believe Israel is committing genocide in Gaza, including 77% of Democrats, while 64% of Republicans disagree. Additionally, 60% of voters oppose sending more U.S. military aid to Israel, with 37% sympathizing more with Palestinians and 36% with Israelis.[^cite_note-646]

## Impact



Millions of people around the world protested Israel's actions, many of them explicitly calling for the recognition and cessation of genocide.

[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw2025b10-647]

British sociologist [Martin Shaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Shaw_\(sociologist\)) has called the Gaza genocide "the genocide that changed the world" because of mass mobilisation against genocide. He also highlights some political victories by Israel, but "at huge cost to [its global legitimacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legitimacy_of_Israel) and that of the West".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShaw2025b4,_14-648] Israeli journalist and author [Gideon Levy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gideon_Levy) wrote an article in *Haaretz* insisting that the people of Gaza will never forget the massacres, bombardments, destruction, and trauma.[^cite_note-649]

Scholars, experts, and other commentators have described the genocide as the "graveyard of [international law](8195726.md)".[^cite_note-650][^cite_note-651][^cite_note-652] Some scholars, particularly those associated with [Third World approaches to international law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_World_approaches_to_international_law), have argued that the international community's failure to treat Israel's actions in Gaza as a genocide and respond accordingly has harmed the principles of the [international order](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_order) and international law and exposed the deficiencies of international governance.[^cite_note-Baars_2024-445][^cite_note-Barreto_2024-446][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEnglertBhattacharyya2024-653] José Manuel Barreto argues that "the Palestinian genocide has unveiled the deep colonial structure of the international legal order", placing the Gaza genocide in a long list of colonial genocides the international system has tolerated.[^cite_note-Barreto_2024-446] Historian [Mark LeVine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_LeVine) argues that Israel's actions have destroyed respect for international law and weakened the taboo against large-scale violence in response to perceived threats.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELeVine20255-654]

Dutch lawyer and professor of international law [André Nollkaemper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9_Nollkaemper?action=edit\&redlink=1) has said that the United States' rejection of international law is a unique feature of the Gaza war.[^cite_note-655] The journalist Colin Jones interviewed lawyers affiliated with the US military and concluded that they see Gaza as a test case for what military conduct might be acceptable in a hypothetical war between the US and a peer power such as China.[^cite_note-Jones_2025-459]

During the [2026 Iran war](82533582.md), Benjamin Netanyahu and Itamar Ben-Gvir both made references to [Amalek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amalek).[^cite_note-656][^cite_note-Malekafzali-657] According to Séamus Malekafzali, this represents an extension of Israel's "genocidal logic" from Gaza to its war on Iran.[^cite_note-Malekafzali-657] In *The Guardian*, Ben Reiff warned that after "the world failed to prevent genocide in Gaza", Israel will employ similar tactics in the [2026 Lebanon war](82549840.md).[^cite_note-658] Sonia Boulos and Raz Segal write that the US and Israel's war on Iran "underlines how genocidal regimes pose a threat on a global scale."[^cite_note-659]

[^cite_note-GHM-15]: &#x20;Overall deaths since 7 October 2023 per the [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md),<sup>[\[5\]](#cite_note-snapshot-6)</sup> which has been deemed reliable by independent organisations<sup>[\[6\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGómez-UgarteChenAcostaBasellini20252-7)</sup><sup>[\[7\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaad2024-8)</sup><sup>[\[8\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEProthero2024-9)</sup><sup>[\[9\]](#cite_note-Debre-10)</sup> and Israeli security officials.<sup>[\[10\]](#cite_note-11)</sup><sup>[\[11\]](#cite_note-12)</sup><sup>[\[12\]](#cite_note-13)</sup> The Ministry's public totals are aggregate and include both civilians and combatants.<sup>[\[9\]](#cite_note-Debre-10)</sup><sup>[\[13\]](#cite_note-14)</sup>

[^cite_note-33]: &#x20; [Amnesty International](18947898.md),<sup>[\[26\]](#cite_note-Amnesty_International_Dec_2024-28)</sup> [Médecins Sans Frontières](20498.md),<sup>[\[27\]](#cite_note-29)</sup> [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem),<sup>[\[28\]](#cite_note-30)</sup> [Physicians for Human Rights–Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Physicians_for_Human_Rights%E2%80%93Israel),<sup>[\[29\]](#cite_note-Kottasová_&_Salman-31)</sup> [International Federation for Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Federation_for_Human_Rights).<sup>[\[30\]](#cite_note-FIDH_2023-32)</sup>

[^cite_note-94]: &#x20;A small number of these deaths (it is unclear exactly how many) were a result of [friendly fire](202484.md), including civilians killed by Israeli military fire while attempting to prevent their abduction by armed Palestinians and combatants killed as a result of the [Hannibal Directive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hannibal_Directive).<sup>[\[85\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem20255-88)</sup><sup>[\[86\]](#cite_note-Ynet240111-89)</sup><sup>[\[87\]](#cite_note-YA240112-90)</sup> There are up to 15 known civilian deaths from these causes: up to 13 in Be'eri, one from helicopter gunfire on a vehicle carrying a hostage,<sup>[\[88\]](#cite_note-91)</sup> and one in Kibbutz Alumim.<sup>[\[89\]](#cite_note-92)</sup> According to [Ynet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ynet), there was an "immense and complex quantity" of friendly-fire incidents on the part of IDF during the 7 October attack, accounting for a fifth of the 105 military deaths then recorded.<sup>[\[90\]](#cite_note-YnetFF-93)</sup>

[^cite_note-147]: &#x20;[Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Hebrew): ״הוריתי להטיל מצור מוחלט על רצועת עזה. לא יהיה חשמל, לא יהיה מזון, לא יהיה דלק הכל סגור. אנחנו נלחמים בחיות אדם ואנחנו נוהגים בהתאם״

[^cite_note-186]: &#x20;Per the [Gaza Health Ministry](52186562.md) and Government Information Office by 3 January 2024, over 22,300 people had been confirmed dead.<sup>[\[180\]](#cite_note-cas1tmp4-185)</sup>

[^cite_note-190]: &#x20;47.4% were non-elderly men, 30.7% were children, 17.1% were non-elderly women, 3.0% were elderly men and 1.9% were elderly women.

[^cite_note-205]: &#x20;In the original Hebrew article the Haaretz reporter used the term "Extermination Zones" (שטחי ההשמדה, *Shtachei Hashmada*)<sup>[\[197\]](#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKubovich2024b-204)</sup> instead of the English version term "Kill Zone".

[^cite_note-240]: &#x20;Per the IPC manual, the starvation death rate does not have to be that high (at least 2 deaths per 10,000 people per day) to declare catastrophic (level 5) food insecurity, but it does need to be that high to declare famine.

[^cite_note-282]: &#x20;Some Israeli officials denied the plan had been adopted; however, an official familiar with the situation stated that some aspects of the plan were already in progress.<sup>[\[272\]](#cite_note-281)</sup>

[^cite_note-Article2GC-324]: &#x20;Article II of the [Genocide Convention](143988.md) defines genocide as "any of the following acts committed with [intent to destroy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genocidal_intent), in whole or in part, a national, ethnical, racial or religious group" through acts such as **(a)** murder, **(b)** inflicting serious bodily or mental harm, **(c)** inflicting conditions of life calculated to destroy the group, **(d)** imposing measures intended to prevent births, and **(e)** forcibly transferring children of the group to another group.<sup>[\[313\]](#cite_note-323)</sup>

[^cite_note-368]: &#x20;The use of white phosphorus against military targets located among civilians is contrary to [Protocol III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protocol_on_Incendiary_Weapons) of the [Convention on Certain Conventional Weapons](3111840.md), of which Israel is not a signatory.<sup>[\[356\]](#cite_note-367)</sup>

It assesses allegations of violations and crimes under international law by Israel in Gaza within the framework of genocide under international law, concluding that there is sufficient evidence to believe that Israel's conduct in Gaza following 7 October 2023 amounts to genocide."

[^cite_note-29]: &#x20;[Médecins Sans Frontières 2025](#CITEREFMédecins_Sans_Frontières2025): "Our decision to describe what's happening in Gaza as a 'genocide' is based on nearly two years of extensive, firsthand information from our teams, who are witnessing massive levels of death and destruction by Israeli forces, a campaign of ethnic cleansing and the almost total dismantling of the health care system."

[^cite_note-KhatibMcKeeYusuf-192]: &#x20;[Khatib, McKee & Yusuf 2024](#CITEREFKhatibMcKeeYusuf2024): "Armed conflicts have indirect health implications beyond the direct harm from violence. Even if the conflict ends immediately, there will continue to be many indirect deaths in the coming months and years from causes such as reproductive, communicable, and non-communicable diseases. The total death toll is expected to be large given the intensity of this conflict; destroyed health-care infrastructure; severe shortages of food, water, and shelter; the population's inability to flee to safe places; and the loss of funding to UNRWA, one of the very few humanitarian organisations still active in the Gaza Strip. In recent conflicts, such indirect deaths range from three to 15 times the number of direct deaths. Applying a conservative estimate of four indirect deaths per one direct death to the 37,396 deaths reported, it is not implausible to estimate that up to 186,000 or even more deaths could be attributable to the current conflict in Gaza. Using the 2022 Gaza Strip population estimate of 2,375,259, this would translate to 7.9% of the total population in the Gaza Strip."

["Report: In One Year, More Than 100,000 Deaths in Gaza—Aided by \$17.9 Billion From the US"](https://web.archive.org/web/20241010053644/https://www.motherjones.com/politics/2024/10/us-israel-funding-gaza-palestine-deaths-october-100000-17-billion/). *[Mother Jones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mother_Jones_\(magazine\))*. Archived from [the original](https://www.motherjones.com/politics/2024/10/us-israel-funding-gaza-palestine-deaths-october-100000-17-billion/) on 10 October 2024. Retrieved 17 October 2024. Brown University's Costs of War Project calculated "the money that's spent on war, and the toll on human lives" after a year of war in Gaza. The numbers are staggering.

[^cite_note-WatsonInstituteEstimatedDeaths-242]: &#x20;[Stamatopoulou-Robbins 2024](#CITEREFStamatopoulou-Robbins2024): "In addition to killing people directly through traumatic injuries, wars cause "indirect deaths" by destroying, damaging, or causing deterioration of economic, social, psychological and health conditions. Most expansively, this report describes the causal pathways that can be expected to lead to far larger numbers of indirect deaths. These deaths result from diseases and other population-level health effects that stem from war's destruction of public infrastructure and livelihood sources, reduced access to water and sanitation, environmental damage, and other such factors. This report builds on a foundation of previous Costs of War research for its framework and methodology in covering the most significant chains of impact, or causal pathways, to indirect war deaths in Gaza and the West Bank. Unlike in combat, these deaths do not necessarily occur immediately or in the close aftermath of the battles which many observers focus on. While it will take years to assess the full extent of these population-level health effects, they will inevitably lead to far higher numbers of deaths than direct violence."

[^cite_note-GazaHealthcareLettersEstimatedDeaths-243]: &#x20;["Appendix to letter of October 2, 2024 re: American physicians observations from the Gaza Strip since October 7, 2023"](https://web.archive.org/web/20241007173405/https://static1.squarespace.com/static/66e083452b3cbf4bbd719aa2/t/66fcd754b472610b6335d66f/1727846228615/Appendix+20241002.pdf) (PDF). *Gaza Healthcare Letters*. 2 October 2024. Archived from [the original](https://static1.squarespace.com/static/66e083452b3cbf4bbd719aa2/t/66fcd754b472610b6335d66f/1727846228615/Appendix+20241002.pdf) (PDF) on 7 October 2024. Retrieved 17 October 2024. These are the most conservative estimates of the death toll that can be made with the given available data as of September 30, 2024. It is highly likely that the real number of deaths in Gaza from this conflict is far higher than this most conservative estimate. Without an immediate ceasefire the death toll will only continue to mount, especially among young children.

[^cite_note-hrw-report-334]: &#x20;[Extermination and Acts of Genocide: Israel Deliberately Depriving Palestinians in Gaza of Water](https://www.hrw.org/report/2024/12/19/extermination-and-acts-genocide/israel-deliberately-depriving-palestinians-gaza) (Report). [Human Rights Watch](46530.md). 19 December 2024. [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20250209092600/https://www.hrw.org/report/2024/12/19/extermination-and-acts-genocide/israel-deliberately-depriving-palestinians-gaza) from the original on 9 February 2025. Retrieved 20 January 2025. Israeli authorities' and forces' actions to deprive the population of Gaza of access to water amount to acts of genocide under the Genocide Convention and the Rome Statute of the International Criminal Court. Specifically, their actions amount to deliberately inflicting conditions of life calculated to bring about the physical destruction of the Palestinian population in Gaza. Genocidal intent may also be inferred from Israeli authorities' and forces' continued actions to deprive Palestinians in Gaza of water, despite clear data and warnings from the United Nations since October and orders from the International Court of Justice calling for the provision of water since January, alongside Israeli authorities' statements, and therefore these acts may amount to the crime of genocide.

[^cite_note-Independent_International_Commission_Inquiry_2025_births-345]: &#x20;[Independent International Commission of Inquiry 2025](#CITEREFIndependent_International_Commission_of_Inquiry2025), p. 39: "175. The Commission finds that the ISF intentionally attacked and destroyed the Basma IVF clinic which was the main fertility centre in Gaza. The ISF destroyed all of the reproductive material that was stored for the future conception of Palestinians. The Commission did not find any evidence that this IVF clinic was a legitimate military target at the time that it was attacked by the ISF. The Commission concludes that the destruction of the Basma IVF clinic was a measure intended to prevent births among Palestinians in Gaza, which is a genocidal act under the Rome Statute and Genocide Convention. The Commission also concludes that this was done with the intent to destroy the Palestinians in Gaza as a group, in whole or in part, and that this is the only inference that could reasonably be drawn from the acts in question"

Years of US and Western [aid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aid) and support to Israel have allowed Israel to develop the infrastructural capabilities to carry out a genocide ... In terms of concealment, political, media, social and cultural [institutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institution) are fully mobilised in the West to hide and conceal this act of genocide, presenting it as a righteous violence of a [victimised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victimised) [Jewish people](25955086.md) everywhere in [the world](9228.md).
